             'urine</li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row"><a '
             'href="Differential_diagnosis" title="Differential diagnosis">Differential '
             'diagnosis</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><div class="hlist"><ul><li><a '
             'href="Dementia_with_Lewy_bodies" title="Dementia with Lewy bodies">Dementia with '
             'Lewy bodies</a></li><li><a href="Progressive_supranuclear_palsy" title="Progressive '
             'supranuclear palsy">progressive supranuclear palsy</a></li><li><a '
             'href="Essential_tremor" title="Essential tremor">essential tremor</a></li><li><a '
             'href="Antipsychotic" title="Antipsychotic">antipsychotic</a> use </li><li><a '
             'href="Fragile_X-associated_tremor/ataxia_syndrome" title="Fragile X-associated '
             'tremor/ataxia syndrome">fragile X-associated tremor/ataxia syndrome</a></li><li><a '
             'href="Huntington\'s_disease" title="Huntington\'s disease">Huntington\'s '
             'disease</a></li><li><a href="Dopamine-responsive_dystonia" '
             'title="Dopamine-responsive dystonia">dopamine-responsive dystonia</a></li><li><a '
             'href="Wilson\'s_disease" title="Wilson\'s disease">Wilson\'s '
             'disease</a></li></ul></div> </td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">Treatment</th><td class="infobox-data">Supportive measures &amp; control '
             'of symptoms, <a href="Physical_therapy" title="Physical therapy">physical '
             'therapy</a>, <a href="Deep_brain_stimulation" title="Deep brain stimulation">deep '
             'brain stimulation</a>, medication</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row"><a href="Medication" title="Medication">Medication</a></th><td '
             'class="infobox-data"><a href="Levodopa" title="Levodopa">Levodopa</a>, <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="COMT_inhibitor" title="COMT inhibitor">COMT '
             'inhibitors</a>, <a href="Aromatic_L-amino_acid_decarboxylase_inhibitor" '
             'title="Aromatic L-amino acid decarboxylase inhibitor">AAAD inhibitors</a>, <a '
             'href="Dopamine_agonist" title="Dopamine agonist">dopamine agonists</a>, <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="MAO-B_inhibitor" title="MAO-B inhibitor">MAO-B '
             'inhibitors</a></td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row"><a '
             'href="Prognosis" title="Prognosis">Prognosis</a></th><td class="infobox-data">No '
             'known cure; near-normal life expectancy.</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">Frequency</th><td class="infobox-data">0.2% (Canada)</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">Named after</th><td class="infobox-data">James '
             "Parkinson</td></tr></tbody></table> <p><b>Parkinson's disease</b> (<b>PD</b>), or "
             'simply <b>Parkinson\'s</b>, is a <a href="Neurodegenerative_disease" '
             'title="Neurodegenerative disease">neurodegenerative disease</a> primarily of the <a '
             'href="Central_nervous_system" title="Central nervous system">central nervous '
             'system</a>, affecting both <a href="Motor_system" title="Motor system">motor</a> and '
             'non-motor systems. The motor symptoms are collectively called <a href="Parkinsonism" '
             'title="Parkinsonism">parkinsonism</a> and include <a href="Tremor" '
             'title="Tremor">tremors</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Bradykinesia" '
             'title="Bradykinesia">bradykinesia</a> (slowness in inititating movement), <a '
             'href="Spasticity" title="Spasticity">rigidity</a>, and <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Postural_instability" title="Postural instability">postural instability</a> '
             '(difficulty maintaining balance). Non-motor symptoms such as <a href="Dysautonomia" '
             'title="Dysautonomia">dysautonomia</a> (<a href="Autonomic_nervous_system" '
             'title="Autonomic nervous system">autonomic nervous system</a> failures), <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Sleep_abnormalities" title="Sleep abnormalities">sleep '
             'abnormalities</a>, <a href="Anosmia" title="Anosmia">anosmia</a> (decreased ability '
             'to smell), and <a href="Behavior_change_(individual)" title="Behavior change '
             '(individual)">behavioral changes</a> or <a href="Mental_disorder" title="Mental '
             'disorder">neuropsychiatric problems</a>, such as <a href="Cognitive_impairment" '
             'title="Cognitive impairment">cognitive impairment</a>, <a href="Psychosis" '
             'title="Psychosis">psychosis</a>, and <a href="Anxiety" title="Anxiety">anxiety</a>, '
             'may appear at any stage of the disease. Symptoms typically develop gradually and '
             'non-motor issues become more prevalent as the disease progresses.  </p><p>Most '
             'Parkinson\'s disease cases are <a href="Idiopathic_disease" title="Idiopathic '
             'disease">idiopathic</a>, though contributing factors have been identified. <a '
             'href="Pathophysiology" title="Pathophysiology">Pathophysiology</a> involves '
             'progressive <a class="mw-redirect" href="Nerve_cell_death" title="Nerve cell '
             'death">degeneration of nerve cells</a> in the <a href="Substantia_nigra" '
             'title="Substantia nigra">substantia nigra</a>, a <a href="Midbrain" '
             'title="Midbrain">midbrain</a> region that provides <a href="Dopamine" '
             'title="Dopamine">dopamine</a> to the <a href="Basal_ganglia" title="Basal '
             'ganglia">basal ganglia</a>, a system involved in voluntary <a href="Motor_control" '
             'title="Motor control">motor control</a>. The cause of this cell death is poorly '
             'understood, but involves the aggregation of <a href="Alpha-synuclein" '
             'title="Alpha-synuclein">alpha-synuclein</a> into <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Lewy_bodies" title="Lewy bodies">Lewy bodies</a> within <a href="Neuron" '
             'title="Neuron">neurons</a>. Other potential factors involve <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Genetic_disease" title="Genetic disease">genetic</a> and environmental '
             'influences, medications, lifestyle, and prior health conditions. </p><p>Diagnosis is '
             'primarily based on <a href="Signs_and_symptoms" title="Signs and symptoms">signs and '
             'symptoms</a>, typically motor-related, identified through <a '
             'href="Neurological_examination" title="Neurological examination">neurological '
             'examination</a>. <a href="Medical_imaging" title="Medical imaging">Medical imaging '
             'techniques</a> such as <a href="Positron_emission_tomography" title="Positron '
             'emission tomography">positron emission tomography</a> can support the diagnosis. PD '
             'typically manifests in individuals over 60, with about one percent affected. In '
             'those younger than 50, it is termed "early-onset PD". </p><p>No cure for PD is '
             'known, and treatment focuses on alleviating symptoms. Initial treatment typically '
             'includes <a href="Levodopa" title="Levodopa">levodopa</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="MAO-B_inhibitor" title="MAO-B inhibitor">MAO-B inhibitors</a>, or <a '
             'href="Dopamine_agonist" title="Dopamine agonist">dopamine agonists</a>. As the '
             'disease progresses, these medications become less effective and may cause <a '
             'href="Dyskinesia" title="Dyskinesia">involuntary muscle movements</a>. Diet and '
             'rehabilitation therapies can help improve symptoms. <a href="Deep_brain_stimulation" '
             'title="Deep brain stimulation">Deep brain stimulation</a> is used to manage severe '
             'motor symptoms when drugs are ineffective. Little evidence exists for treatments '
             'addressing non-motor symptoms, such as sleep disturbances and mood instability. Life '
             'expectancy for those with PD is near-normal, but is decreased for early-onset. </p> '
             '<div class="toclimit-3"><meta property="mw:PageProp/toc"/></div> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Classification_and_terminology">Classification and terminology</h2></div> <div '
             'class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">See also: <a '
             'href="Parkinsonism" title="Parkinsonism">Parkinsonism</a> and <a '
             'href="Parkinson-plus_syndrome" title="Parkinson-plus syndrome">Parkinson-plus '
             "syndrome</a></div> <p>Parkinson's disease (PD) is a <a "
             'href="Neurodegenerative_disease" title="Neurodegenerative disease">neurodegenerative '
             'disease</a> affecting both the <a href="Central_nervous_system" title="Central '
             'nervous system">central</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Peripheral_nervous_systems" title="Peripheral nervous systems">peripheral '
             'nervous systems</a>, characterized by the <a href="Cell_death" title="Cell '
             'death">loss</a> of <a href="Dopamine" title="Dopamine">dopamine</a>-producing <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Neurons" title="Neurons">neurons</a> in the <a '
             'href="Substantia_nigra" title="Substantia nigra">substantia nigra</a> region of the '
             'brain. It is classified as a <a href="Synucleinopathy" '
             'title="Synucleinopathy">synucleinopathy</a> due to the abnormal accumulation of the '
             'protein <a href="Alpha-synuclein" title="Alpha-synuclein">alpha-synuclein</a>, which '
             'aggregates into <a class="mw-redirect" href="Lewy_bodies" title="Lewy bodies">Lewy '
             'bodies</a> within affected neurons. </p><p>The loss of dopamine-producing neurons in '
             "the substantia nigra causes movement abnormalities, leading to Parkinson's further "
             'categorization as a <a href="Movement_disorder" title="Movement disorder">movement '
             'disorder</a>. In 30% of cases, disease progression leads to the cognitive decline, '
             'resulting in <a href="Parkinson\'s_disease_dementia" title="Parkinson\'s disease '
             'dementia">Parkinson\'s disease dementia</a> (PDD). Alongside <a '
             'href="Dementia_with_Lewy_bodies" title="Dementia with Lewy bodies">dementia with '
             'Lewy bodies</a>, PDD is one of the two subtypes of <a href="Lewy_body_dementia" '
             'title="Lewy body dementia">Lewy body dementia</a>. </p><p>The four cardinal motor '
             'symptoms of Parkinson\'s—<a class="mw-redirect" href="Bradykinesia" '
             'title="Bradykinesia">bradykinesia</a> (slowed movements), <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Postural_instability" title="Postural instability">postural instability</a>, '
             '<a href="Spasticity" title="Spasticity">rigidity</a>, and <a href="Tremor" '
             'title="Tremor">tremor</a>—are called <a href="Parkinsonism" '
             'title="Parkinsonism">parkinsonism</a>. These four symptoms are not exclusive to '
             "Parkinson's and can occur in many other conditions, including <a "
             'class="mw-redirect" href="HIV_infection" title="HIV infection">HIV infection</a> and '
             'recreational drug use. Neurodegenerative diseases that feature parkinsonism, but '
             'have distinct differences are grouped under the umbrella of <a '
             'href="Parkinson-plus_syndrome" title="Parkinson-plus syndrome">Parkinson-plus '
             'syndromes</a>, or alternatively, atypical parkinsonian disorders. PD can be '
             'attributed to <a href="Genetic_disorder" title="Genetic disorder">genetic '
             'factors</a>, but most cases are <a href="Idiopathic_disease" title="Idiopathic '
             'disease">idiopathic</a>, with no clearly identifiable cause. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Signs_and_symptoms">Signs and '
             'symptoms</h2></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main '
             'article: <a href="Signs_and_symptoms_of_Parkinson\'s_disease" title="Signs and '
             'symptoms of Parkinson\'s disease">Signs and symptoms of Parkinson\'s '
             'disease</a></div> <p>A wide spectrum of motor and non-motor symptoms appears in '
             'Parkinson\'s disease. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Motor">Motor</h3></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" '
             'role="note">See also: <a href="Parkinsonism" '
             'title="Parkinsonism">Parkinsonism</a></div> <div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div '
             'class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:192px;max-width:192px"><div '
             'class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:190px;max-width:190px"><div '
             'class="thumbimage" style="height:192px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><img '
             'alt="The stooped posture of a patient with Parkinson\'s" class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="1106" data-file-width="1080" decoding="async" height="193" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Paralysis_agitans-Male_Parkinson\'s_victim-1892_cropped.png" '
             'width="188"/></span></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" '
             'style="width:190px;max-width:190px"><div class="thumbimage" '
             'style="height:73px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="The small, '
             'jagged handwriting of a PD patient" class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="845" '
             'data-file-width="2175" decoding="async" height="73" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Writing_by_a_Parkinson\'s_disease_patient.png" '
             'width="188"/></span></div></div></div><div class="trow" style="display:flex"><div '
             'class="thumbcaption">Motor symptoms include a stooping posture, the <a '
             'href="Parkinsonian_gait" title="Parkinsonian gait">Parkinsonian gait</a>, and <a '
             'href="Micrographia_(handwriting)" title="Micrographia '
             '(handwriting)">micrographia</a>—jagged, diminutive '
             'handwriting.</div></div></div></div> <p>The cardinal motor features are tremor, '
             'bradykinesia, rigidity, and postural instability, collectively termed <a '
             'href="Parkinsonism" title="Parkinsonism">parkinsonism</a>. Appearing in 70–75% of '
             'those with PD, tremor is often the predominant motor symptom. Resting tremor is the '
             'most common, but kinetic tremors—occurring during voluntary movements—and postural '
             'tremor—preventing upright, stable posture—also occur. Tremor largely affects the '
             'hands and feet: a classic parkinsonian tremor is "<a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Pill-rolling" title="Pill-rolling">pill-rolling</a>", a resting tremor in '
             'which the thumb and index finger make contact in a circular motion at 4–6 Hz '
             'frequency. </p><p>Bradykinesia describes difficulties in <a href="Motor_planning" '
             'title="Motor planning">motor planning</a>, beginning, and executing, resulting in '
             'overall slowed movement with reduced amplitude that affects sequential and '
             'simultaneous tasks. Bradykinesia can also lead to <a href="Hypomimia" '
             'title="Hypomimia">hypomimia</a>, reduced facial expressions. <a href="Spasticity" '
             'title="Spasticity">Rigidity</a>, also called rigor, refers to a feeling of stiffness '
             'and resistance to passive stretching of muscles. <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Postural_instability" title="Postural instability">Postural instability</a> '
             'typically appears in later stages, leading to <a href="Balance_disorder" '
             'title="Balance disorder">impaired balance</a> and <a href="Falls_in_older_adults" '
             'title="Falls in older adults">falls</a>. Postural instability also leads to a '
             'forward stooping posture. </p><p>Beyond the cardinal four, other motor deficits, '
             'termed secondary motor symptoms, commonly occur. Notably, gait disturbances result '
             'in the <a href="Parkinsonian_gait" title="Parkinsonian gait">parkinsonian gait</a>, '
             'which includes shuffling and <a href="Paroxysmal_dyskinesia" title="Paroxysmal '
             'dyskinesia">paroxysmal deficits</a>, where a normal gait is interrupted by rapid '
             'footsteps—known as festination—or sudden stops, impairing balance and causing falls. '
             'Most people with PD experience speech problems, including <a href="Stuttering" '
             'title="Stuttering">stuttering</a>, <a href="Hypophonia" '
             'title="Hypophonia">hypophonic, "soft" speech</a>, <a href="Dysarthria" '
             'title="Dysarthria">slurring</a>, and festinating speech (rapid and poorly '
             'intelligible). Handwriting is commonly altered in PD, decreasing in size—known as <a '
             'href="Micrographia_(handwriting)" title="Micrographia '
             '(handwriting)">micrographia</a>—and becoming jagged and sharply fluctuating. Grip '
             'and dexterity are also impaired. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Neuropsychiatric_and_cognitive">Neuropsychiatric and cognitive</h3></div> <p><a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Neuropsychiatric" '
             'title="Neuropsychiatric">Neuropsychiatric</a> symptoms such as <a href="Anxiety" '
             'title="Anxiety">anxiety</a>, apathy, <a href="Depression_(mood)" title="Depression '
             '(mood)">depression</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Hallucinations" '
             'title="Hallucinations">hallucination</a>, and <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Impulse_control_disorders" title="Impulse control disorders">impulse control '
             'disorders</a> occur in up to 60% of those with PD. They often precede motor '
             'symptoms, vary with disease progression, and may appear at any stage of the disease '
             'including the <a href="Prodrome" title="Prodrome">prodromal phase</a>. Non-motor '
             'fluctuations, including dysphoria, <a href="Fatigue" title="Fatigue">fatigue</a>, '
             'and slowness of thought, are also common. Some neuropsychiatric symptoms are not '
             'directly caused by neurodegeneration, but rather by its pharmacological management. '
             '</p> <table class="wikitable" style="float:right; margin-left:1em; font-size:90%; '
             'line-height:1.4em; width:400px;"> <caption><span class="sr-only">Table featuring the '
             'prevalence of neuropsychiatric symptoms in PD</span> </caption> '
             "<caption>Neuropsychiatric symptom prevalence in Parkinson's disease </caption> "
             '<tbody><tr> <th rowspan="2" '
             'style="background:#33D2FD;color:black;text-align:center;">Symptom </th></tr> <tr> '
             '<th style="background:#33D2FD;color:black;">Prevalence (%) </th></tr> <tr> <th><a '
             'href="Anxiety" title="Anxiety">Anxiety</a> </th> <td '
             'style="text-align:center;">40–50 </td></tr> <tr> <th>Apathy </th> <td '
             'style="text-align:center;">40 </td></tr> <tr> <th><a href="Depression_(mood)" '
             'title="Depression (mood)">Depression</a> </th> <td style="text-align:center;">20–40 '
             '</td></tr> <tr> <th><a class="mw-redirect" href="Impulse_control_disorders" '
             'title="Impulse control disorders">Impulse control disorders</a> </th> <td '
             'style="text-align:center;">36–60 </td></tr> <tr> <th><a href="Psychosis" '
             'title="Psychosis">Psychosis</a> </th> <td style="text-align:center;"> </td></tr> '
             '</tbody></table> <p>Cognitive impairments rank among the most prevalent and '
             'debilitating non-motor symptoms. These deficits may emerge in the early stages or '
             'before diagnosis, and their prevalence and severity tend to increase with disease '
             'progression. Ranging from <a href="Mild_cognitive_impairment" title="Mild cognitive '
             'impairment">mild cognitive impairment</a> to severe <a '
             'href="Parkinson\'s_disease_dementia" title="Parkinson\'s disease '
             'dementia">Parkinson\'s disease dementia</a>, these impairments include <a '
             'href="Executive_dysfunction" title="Executive dysfunction">executive '
             'dysfunction</a>, <a href="Bradyphrenia" title="Bradyphrenia">slowed cognitive '
             'processing speed</a>, and disruptions in time perception and estimation. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Autonomic">Autonomic</h3></div> <p><a '
             'href="Autonomic_nervous_system" title="Autonomic nervous system">Autonomic nervous '
             'system</a> failures, known as <a href="Dysautonomia" '
             'title="Dysautonomia">dysautonomia</a>, can appear at any stage of PD. They are among '
             'the most debilitating symptoms and greatly reduce quality of life. Although almost '
             'all individuals with PD have cardiovascular autonomic dysfunction, only some are '
             'symptomatic. Chiefly, <a href="Orthostatic_hypotension" title="Orthostatic '
             'hypotension">orthostatic hypotension</a>—a sustained <a href="Blood_pressure" '
             'title="Blood pressure">blood pressure</a> drop of at least 20 mmHg <a href="Systole" '
             'title="Systole">systolic</a> or 10 mmHg <a class="mw-redirect" href="Diastolic" '
             'title="Diastolic">diastolic</a> after standing—occurs in 30–50% of cases. This can '
             'result in <a href="Lightheadedness" title="Lightheadedness">lightheadedness</a> or '
             '<a href="Syncope_(medicine)" title="Syncope (medicine)">fainting</a>; subsequent '
             'falls are associated with higher morbidity and mortality. </p><p>Other autonomic '
             'failures include <a class="mw-redirect" href="Gastrointestinal_issues" '
             'title="Gastrointestinal issues">gastrointestinal issues</a> such as chronic '
             'constipation, <a href="Gastroparesis" title="Gastroparesis">impaired stomach '
             'emptying</a> and subsequent <a href="Nausea" title="Nausea">nausea</a>, <a '
             'href="Hypersalivation" title="Hypersalivation">excessive salivation</a>, and <a '
             'href="Dysphagia" title="Dysphagia">dysphagia</a> (difficulty swallowing); all '
             'greatly reduce quality of life. Dysphagia, for instance, can prevent pill swallowing '
             'and lead to <a href="Aspiration_pneumonia" title="Aspiration pneumonia">aspiration '
             'pneumonia</a>. <a href="Urinary_incontinence" title="Urinary incontinence">Urinary '
             'incontinence</a>, <a href="Sexual_dysfunction" title="Sexual dysfunction">sexual '
             'dysfunction</a>, and <a href="Thermoregulation" '
             'title="Thermoregulation">thermoregulatory dysfunction</a>—including heat and cold '
             'intolerance and excessive sweating—also frequently occur. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Other">Other</h3></div> <p>Sensory deficits '
             'appear in up to 90% of people with PD and are usually present at early stages. <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Nociceptive_pain" title="Nociceptive pain">Nociceptive</a> '
             'and <a href="Neuropathic_pain" title="Neuropathic pain">neuropathic pain</a> are '
             'common, with <a href="Peripheral_neuropathy" title="Peripheral '
             'neuropathy">peripheral neuropathy</a> affecting up to 55% of individuals. <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Visual_impairments" title="Visual impairments">Visual '
             'impairments</a> are also frequently observed, including deficits in <a '
             'href="Visual_acuity" title="Visual acuity">visual acuity</a>, color vision, <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Eye_coordination" title="Eye coordination">eye '
             'coordination</a>, and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Visual_hallucinations" '
             'title="Visual hallucinations">visual hallucinations</a>. An <a href="Anosmia" '
             'title="Anosmia">impaired sense of smell</a> is also prevalent. Individuals often '
             'struggle with spatial awareness, recognizing faces and emotions, and may experience '
             'challenges with reading and double vision. </p><p><a href="Sleep_disorder" '
             'title="Sleep disorder">Sleep disorders</a> are highly prevalent in PD, affecting up '
             'to 98%. These disorders include <a href="Insomnia" title="Insomnia">insomnia</a>, <a '
             'href="Excessive_daytime_sleepiness" title="Excessive daytime sleepiness">excessive '
             'daytime sleepiness</a>, <a href="Restless_legs_syndrome" title="Restless legs '
             'syndrome">restless legs syndrome</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="REM_sleep_behavior_disorder" title="REM sleep behavior disorder">REM sleep '
             'behavior disorder</a> (RBD), and <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Sleep-disordered_breathing" title="Sleep-disordered '
             'breathing">sleep-disordered breathing</a>, many of which can be worsened by '
             'medication. RBD may begin years before the initial motor symptoms. Individual '
             'presentation of symptoms varies, although most people affected by PD show an altered '
             '<a href="Circadian_rhythm" title="Circadian rhythm">circadian rhythm</a> at some '
             'point of disease progression. </p><p>PD is also associated with a variety of <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Skin_disorder" title="Skin disorder">skin disorders</a> '
             'that include <a href="Melanoma" title="Melanoma">melanoma</a>, <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Seborrheic_dermatitis" title="Seborrheic '
             'dermatitis">seborrheic dermatitis</a>, <a href="Bullous_pemphigoid" title="Bullous '
             'pemphigoid">bullous pemphigoid</a>, and <a href="Rosacea" '
             'title="Rosacea">rosacea</a>. Seborrheic dermatitis is recognized as a premotor '
             'feature that indicates dysautonomia and demonstrates that PD can be detected not '
             'only by changes of <a href="Nervous_tissue" title="Nervous tissue">nervous '
             'tissue</a>, but also tissue abnormalities outside the nervous system. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Causes">Causes</h2></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Causes_of_Parkinson\'s_disease" title="Causes of Parkinson\'s disease">Causes '
             "of Parkinson's disease</a></div> <p>As of 2024, the cause of neurodegeneration in PD "
             'is unclear, though it is believed to result from the interplay of <a href="Genetics" '
             'title="Genetics">genetic</a> and <a href="Environmental_factor" title="Environmental '
             'factor">environmental</a> factors. Most cases are <a href="Idiopathic_disease" '
             'title="Idiopathic disease">idiopathic</a> with no clearly identifiable cause, while '
             'about 5–10% are familial. Around a third of familial cases can be attributed to a '
             'single monogenic cause. </p><p>Molecularly, abnormal aggregation of alpha-synuclein '
             'is considered a key contributor to PD <a href="Pathogenesis" '
             'title="Pathogenesis">pathogenesis</a>, although the trigger for this aggregation is '
             'debated and some forms of PD do not include these aggregations. Also, the '
             'vulnerability of substantia nigra pars compacta (SNc) dopaminergic neurons to '
             'oxidative stress, caused in part by intracellular dopamine being toxic, has been '
             'proposed as a major contributor to the disease. <a href="Proteostasis" '
             'title="Proteostasis">Proteostasis</a> disruption and the dysfunction of cell '
             'organelles, including <a class="mw-redirect" href="Endosomes" '
             'title="Endosomes">endosomes</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Lysosomes" '
             'title="Lysosomes">lysosomes</a>, and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Mitochondria" '
             'title="Mitochondria">mitochondria</a>, are implicated in pathogenesis. Additionally, '
             'maladaptive immune and inflammatory responses are potential contributors. The '
             'substantial heterogeneity in PD presentation and progression suggests the '
             'involvement of multiple interacting triggers and pathogenic pathways. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Genetic">Genetic</h3></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="A ribbon diagram of the '
             'protein parkin" class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="379" data-file-width="614" '
             'decoding="async" height="154" loading="lazy" src="./Protein_PARK2_PDB_1iyf.png" '
             'width="250"/><figcaption>Ribbon diagram of <a href="Parkin_(protein)" title="Parkin '
             '(protein)">parkin</a></figcaption></figure> <p>Parkinson\'s can be narrowly defined '
             'as a genetic disease, as rare inherited gene variants have been firmly linked to '
             'monogenic PD, and most cases carry variants that increase PD risk. PD <a '
             'href="Heritability" title="Heritability">heritability</a> is estimated to range from '
             '22 to 40%. Around 15% of diagnosed individuals have a <a '
             'href="Family_history_(medicine)" title="Family history (medicine)">family '
             'history</a>, of which 5–10% can be attributed to a causative risk gene <a '
             'href="Mutation" title="Mutation">mutation</a>. Carrying one of these mutations may '
             'not lead to disease. Rates of familial PD vary by ethnicity; monogenic PD occurs in '
             'up to 40% of Arab-Berber and 20% of Ashkenazi Jewish people with PD. </p><p>As of '
             '2024, around 90 genetic risk variants across 78 genomic loci have been identified. '
             'Notable risk variants include <i>SNCA</i> (which encodes alpha-synuclein), '
             '<i>LRRK2</i>, and <i>VPS35</i> for <a class="mw-redirect" href="Autosomal_dominant" '
             'title="Autosomal dominant">autosomal dominant</a> inheritance, and <i>PRKN</i>, '
             '<i>PINK1</i>, and <i>DJ1</i> for <a class="mw-redirect" href="Autosomal_recessive" '
             'title="Autosomal recessive">autosomal recessive</a> inheritance. <i>LRRK2</i> is the '
             'most common autosomal dominant variant, responsible for 1–2% of all PD cases and 40% '
             'of familial cases. <a href="Parkin_(protein)" title="Parkin (protein)">Parkin</a> '
             'variants are associated with nearly half of recessive, early-onset monogenic PD. '
             'Mutations in the <i>GBA1</i> gene, linked to <a href="Gaucher\'s_disease" '
             'title="Gaucher\'s disease">Gaucher\'s disease</a>, can cause monogenic PD, and are '
             'associated with cognitive decline. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Environmental">Environmental</h3></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">See also: <a href="Environmental_health" '
             'title="Environmental health">Environmental health</a> and <a href="Exposome" '
             'title="Exposome">Exposome</a></div> <p>The limited heritability of Parkinson\'s '
             'strongly suggests environmental factors are involved, though identifying these risk '
             "factors and establishing causality is challenging due to PD's decade-long prodromal "
             'period. </p><p>Environmental toxicants such as air pollution, pesticides, and '
             'industrial solvents like trichloroethylene are strongly linked to PD. Certain '
             'pesticides—such as <a href="Paraquat" title="Paraquat">paraquat</a>, glyphosate, and '
             'rotenone—are the most established environmental toxicants for PD and are likely '
             'causal. PD prevalence is strongly associated with local pesticide use, and many '
             'pesticides are mitochondrial toxins. Paraquat, for instance, structurally resembles '
             'metabolized MPTP, which selectively kills dopaminergic neurons by inhibiting <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Mitochondrial_complex_1" title="Mitochondrial complex '
             '1">mitochondrial complex 1</a> and is widely used to model PD. Pesticide exposure '
             'after diagnosis may also accelerate disease progression. Without high pesticide '
             'exposure, an estimated 20% of all PD cases would be prevented. </p><p><a '
             'href="Epidemiology" title="Epidemiology">Epidemiological</a> evidence also supports '
             "the association of pesticide exposure with Parkinson's Disease. For example, a 2025 "
             'study found that people who lived within 1-3 miles of a golf course were at '
             "increased risk for developing Parkinson's Disease, and that people who obtained "
             'their drinking water from service areas containing a golf course had twice the risk '
             'of developing Parkinson\'s disease compared to controls. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Hypotheses">Hypotheses</h3></div> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading4"><h4 id="Prionic">Prionic</h4></div> <p>The hallmark of PD is the '
             'formation of protein aggregates, beginning with alpha-synuclein fibrils and followed '
             'by Lewy bodies and Lewy neurites. The <a href="Prion" title="Prion">prion</a> '
             'hypothesis suggests that alpha-synuclein aggregates are pathogenic and can spread to '
             'neighboring, healthy neurons and seed new aggregates. Some propose that the '
             'heterogeneity of PD may stem from different "strains" of alpha-synuclein aggregates '
             'and varying anatomical sites of origin. Alpha-synuclein propagation has been '
             'demonstrated in cell and animal models and is the most popular explanation for the '
             'progressive spread through specific neuronal systems. However, therapeutic efforts '
             'to clear alpha-synuclein have failed. Additionally, postmortem brain tissue analysis '
             'shows that alpha-synuclein pathology does not clearly progress through the nearest '
             'neural connections. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 '
             'id="Braak\'s">Braak\'s</h4></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" '
             'role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Parkinson\'s_disease_and_gut-brain_axis#Braak\'s_hypothesis" '
             'title="Parkinson\'s disease and gut-brain axis">Parkinson\'s disease and gut-brain '
             "axis §\xa0Braak's hypothesis</a></div> <p>In 2002, Heiko Braak and colleagues "
             'proposed that PD begins outside the brain and is triggered by a "neuroinvasion" of '
             'some unknown pathogen. The pathogen enters through the nasal cavity and is swallowed '
             'into the digestive tract, initiating Lewy pathology in both areas. This '
             'alpha-synuclein pathology may then travel from the gut to the central nervous system '
             'through the <a href="Vagus_nerve" title="Vagus nerve">vagus nerve</a>. This theory '
             'could explain the presence of Lewy pathology in both the enteric nervous system and '
             'olfactory tract neurons, as well as clinical symptoms such as loss of smell and '
             'gastrointestinal problems. Environmental toxicants ingested in a similar manner '
             'might also trigger PD. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Risk_factors">Risk factors</h3></div> <p>As 90% of PD cases are idiopathic, the '
             'identification of the risk factors that may influence disease progression or '
             'severity is critical. The most significant risk factor in developing PD is age, with '
             'a prevalence of 1% in those over 65 and around 4.3% in those over 85. <a '
             'href="Traumatic_brain_injury" title="Traumatic brain injury">Traumatic brain '
             'injury</a> significantly increases PD risk, especially if recent. Dairy consumption '
             'correlates with a higher risk, possibly due to contaminants such as <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Heptachlor_epoxide" title="Heptachlor epoxide">heptachlor '
             'epoxide</a>. Although the connection is unclear, <a href="Melanoma" '
             'title="Melanoma">melanoma</a> diagnosis is associated with a roughly 45% risk '
             'increase. Also, an association was found between <a href="Methamphetamine" '
             'title="Methamphetamine">methamphetamine</a> use and PD risk. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Protective_factors">Protective '
             'factors</h3></div> <p>Although no compounds or activities have been mechanistically '
             'established as <a href="Neuroprotection" title="Neuroprotection">neuroprotective</a> '
             'for PD, several factors have been found to be associated with a decreased risk. <a '
             'href="Tobacco" title="Tobacco">Tobacco use</a> and <a href="Tobacco_smoking" '
             'title="Tobacco smoking">smoking</a> are strongly associated with a decreased risk, '
             'reducing the chance of developing PD by up to 70%. Various tobacco and smoke '
             'components have been hypothesized to be neuroprotective, including <a '
             'href="Nicotine" title="Nicotine">nicotine</a>, carbon monoxide, and <a '
             'href="Monoamine_oxidase_inhibitor" title="Monoamine oxidase inhibitor">monoamine '
             'oxidase B inhibitors</a>. Consumption of <a href="Caffeine" '
             'title="Caffeine">caffeine</a> as an ingredient of coffee or tea is also strongly '
             'associated with neuroprotection. </p><p>Although findings have varied, usage of <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Nonsteroidal_anti-inflammatory_drugs" title="Nonsteroidal '
             'anti-inflammatory drugs">nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs</a> (NSAIDs) such as '
             '<a href="Ibuprofen" title="Ibuprofen">ibuprofen</a> may be neuroprotective. <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Calcium_channel_blockers" title="Calcium channel '
             'blockers">Calcium channel blockers</a> may also have a protective effect, with a 22% '
             'risk reduction reported. Higher blood concentrations of urate—a potent <a '
             'href="Antioxidant" title="Antioxidant">antioxidant</a>—have been proposed to be '
             'neuroprotective. Although longitudinal studies observe a slight decrease in PD risk '
             "among those who consume alcohol—possibly due to alcohol's urate-increasing "
             'effect—alcohol abuse may increase risk. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Pathophysiology">Pathophysiology</h2></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Pathophysiology_of_Parkinson\'s_disease" title="Pathophysiology of '
             'Parkinson\'s disease">Pathophysiology of Parkinson\'s disease</a></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="1350" data-file-width="2250" decoding="async" height="228" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Blausen_0704_ParkinsonsDisease.png" '
             'width="380"/><figcaption>Parkinson\'s results from the death of <a href="Dopamine" '
             'title="Dopamine">dopamine</a>-releasing <a href="Neuron" title="Neuron">neurons</a> '
             'in the <a class="mw-redirect" href="Substantia_nigra_pars_compacta" '
             'title="Substantia nigra pars compacta">substantia nigra pars compacta</a>, seen by '
             'the loss of dark neuromelanin in the lower inset.</figcaption></figure> '
             "<p>Parkinson's disease has two hallmark pathophysiological processes: the abnormal "
             'aggregation of alpha-synuclein that leads to Lewy pathology, and the degeneration of '
             'dopaminergic neurons in the <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Substantia_nigra_pars_compacta" title="Substantia nigra pars '
             'compacta">substantia nigra pars compacta</a>. The death of these neurons reduces '
             'available dopamine in the <a href="Striatum" title="Striatum">striatum</a>, which in '
             'turn affects circuits controlling movement in the <a href="Basal_ganglia" '
             'title="Basal ganglia">basal ganglia</a>. By the time motor symptoms appear, 50–80% '
             'of all dopaminergic neurons in the substantia nigra have degenerated. </p><p>Cell '
             'death and Lewy pathology, though, are not limited to the substantia nigra. The <a '
             'href="Braak_staging" title="Braak staging">six-stage Braak system</a> holds that '
             'alpha-synuclein pathology begins in the <a href="Olfactory_bulb" title="Olfactory '
             'bulb">olfactory bulb</a> or outside the central nervous system in the <a '
             'href="Enteric_nervous_system" title="Enteric nervous system">enteric nervous '
             'system</a> before ascending the brain stem. In the third Braak stage, Lewy body '
             'pathology appears in the substantia nigra, and by the sixth step, Lewy pathology has '
             'spread to the limbic and neocortical regions. Although Braak staging offers a strong '
             'basis for PD progression, around 50% of individuals do not follow the predicted '
             'model. Lewy pathology is highly variable and may be entirely absent in some persons '
             'with PD. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Alpha-synuclein_pathology">Alpha-synuclein pathology</h3></div> <div '
             'class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Further information: <a '
             'href="Protein_aggregation" title="Protein aggregation">Protein aggregation</a> and '
             '<a href="Lewy_body" title="Lewy body">Lewy body</a></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="2096" data-file-width="2800" decoding="async" height="225" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Lewy_bodies_(alpha_synuclein_inclusions).jpg" '
             'width="300"/><figcaption><a class="mw-redirect" href="Lewy_bodies" title="Lewy '
             'bodies">Lewy bodies and Lewy neurites</a> stained brown in PD brain '
             'tissue</figcaption></figure> <p>Alpha-synuclein is an intracellular protein '
             'typically localized to <a class="mw-redirect" href="Presynaptic_terminals" '
             'title="Presynaptic terminals">presynaptic terminals</a> and involved in <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Synaptic_vesicle_trafficking" title="Synaptic vesicle '
             'trafficking">synaptic vesicle trafficking</a>, intracellular transport, and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Neurotransmitter_release" title="Neurotransmitter '
             'release">neurotransmitter release</a>. When misfolded, it can aggregate into '
             'oligomers and protofibrils that in turn lead to Lewy body formation. Due to their '
             'lower molecular weight, oligomers and protofibrils may disseminate and be '
             'transmitted to other cells more rapidly. </p><p>Lewy bodies consist of a fibrillar '
             'exterior and a granular core. Although alpha-synuclein is the dominant <a '
             'class="extiw external" href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/proteinaceous" '
             'title="wikt:proteinaceous">proteinaceous</a> component, the core contains '
             'mitochondrial and autophagosomal membrane components, suggesting a link with '
             'organelle dysfunction. Whether Lewy bodies themselves contribute to or are simply '
             'the result of PD pathogenesis is unclear; alpha-synuclein oligomers can '
             'independently mediate cell damage, and neurodegeneration can precede Lewy body '
             'formation. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Pathways_involved_in_neurodegeneration">Pathways involved in '
             'neurodegeneration</h3></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" '
             'role="note">See also: <a class="mw-redirect" href="Neurodegeneration" '
             'title="Neurodegeneration">Neurodegeneration §\xa0Mechanisms</a></div> <p>Three major '
             'pathways—<a class="mw-redirect" href="Vesicular_trafficking" title="Vesicular '
             'trafficking">vesicular trafficking</a>, <a href="Lysosome" '
             'title="Lysosome">lysosomal degradation</a>, and mitochondrial maintenance—are known '
             'to be affected by and contribute to PD pathogenesis, with all three linked to '
             'alpha-synuclein. High-risk gene variants also impair all three of these processes. '
             'All steps of vesicular trafficking are impaired by alpha-synuclein. It blocks '
             'endoplasmic reticulum (ER) vesicles from reaching the Golgi—leading to ER stress—and '
             'Golgi vesicles from reaching the <a href="Lysosome" title="Lysosome">lysosome</a>, '
             'preventing alpha-synuclein degradation and leading to its build-up. Risky gene '
             'variants, chiefly <i>GBA</i>, further compromise lysosomal function. Although the '
             'mechanism is not well established, alpha-synuclein can impair mitochondrial function '
             'and cause subsequent oxidative stress. Mitochondrial dysfunction can, in turn, lead '
             'to further alpha-synuclein accumulation in a positive feedback loop. Microglial '
             'activation, possibly caused by alpha-synuclein, is also strongly indicated. </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Mitochondrial_dysfunction">Mitochondrial '
             'dysfunction</h4></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" '
             'role="note">See also: <a href="Mitochondrial_disease" title="Mitochondrial '
             'disease">Mitochondrial disease</a></div> <p>Mitochondrial dysfunction is '
             'well-established in PD. Increased oxidative stress and reduced <a '
             'href="Calcium_buffering" title="Calcium buffering">calcium buffering</a> may '
             'contribute to neurodegeneration. The finding that <a href="MPP%2B" '
             'title="MPP+">MPP</a>—a <a href="Respiratory_complex_I" title="Respiratory complex '
             'I">respiratory complex I</a> inhibitor and MPTP metabolite—caused parkinsonian '
             'symptoms strongly implied that mitochondria contributed to PD pathogenesis. '
             'Additionally, faulty gene variants involved in familial PD—including <i>PINK1</i> '
             'and <i>Parkin</i>—prevent the elimination of dysfunctional mitochondria through '
             'mitophagy. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 '
             'id="Neuroinflammation">Neuroinflammation</h4></div> <p>Some hypothesize that '
             'neurodegeneration arises from a chronic <a href="Neuroimmune_system" '
             'title="Neuroimmune system">neuroinflammatory state</a> created by local activated <a '
             'href="Microglia" title="Microglia">microglia</a> and infiltrating immune cells. '
             'Mitochondrial dysfunction may also drive immune activation, particularly in '
             'monogenic PD. Some <a class="mw-redirect" href="Autoimmune_disorders" '
             'title="Autoimmune disorders">autoimmune disorders</a> increase the risk of '
             'developing PD, supporting an autoimmune contribution. Additionally, <a '
             'href="Influenza" title="Influenza">influenza</a> and <a href="Herpes_simplex_virus" '
             'title="Herpes simplex virus">herpes simplex virus</a> infections increase the risk '
             'of PD, possibly due to a <a href="Molecular_mimicry" title="Molecular mimicry">viral '
             'protein resembling</a> alpha-synuclein. PD risk is also decreased with <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Immunosuppressants" '
             'title="Immunosuppressants">immunosuppressants</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Diagnosis">Diagnosis</h2></div> <p>Diagnosis of PD is largely '
             'clinical, relying on <a href="Medical_history" title="Medical history">medical '
             'history</a> and examination of symptoms, with an emphasis on symptoms that appear in '
             'later stages. Although early-stage diagnosis is not reliable, prodromal diagnosis '
             'may consider previous family history of PD and possible early symptoms such as <a '
             'href="Rapid_eye_movement_sleep_behavior_disorder" title="Rapid eye movement sleep '
             'behavior disorder">rapid eye movement sleep behavior disorder</a> (RBD), reduced <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Olfaction" title="Olfaction">sense of smell</a>, and '
             'gastrointestinal issues. Isolated RBD is a particularly significant sign, as 90% of '
             'those affected will develop some form of neurodegenerative parkinsonism. Diagnosis '
             'in later stages requires the manifestation of parkinsonism, specifically '
             'bradykinesia and rigidity or tremor. Further support includes other motor and '
             'non-motor symptoms and genetic profiling. </p><p>A PD diagnosis is typically '
             'confirmed by any two of these criteria: responsiveness to levodopa, resting tremor, '
             'levodopa-induced dyskinesia, or <a href="DaT_scan" title="DaT scan">dopamine '
             'transporter single-proton emission computed tomography</a> confirmation. If these '
             'criteria are not met, atypical parkinsonism is considered. Definitive diagnoses can '
             'only be made <a href="Autopsy" title="Autopsy">post mortem</a> through pathological '
             'analysis. Misdiagnosis is common, with a reported error rate near 25%, and diagnoses '
             'often change during follow-ups. Diagnosis can be further complicated by multiple '
             'overlapping conditions. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Imaging">Imaging</h3></div> <figure class="mw-default-size" '
             'typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="946" '
             'data-file-width="1478" decoding="async" height="224" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Reduced_striatal_18F-DOPA_uptake.png" width="350"/><figcaption>Reduced '
             'radioisotopic <a class="mw-redirect" href="Fluorodopa" title="Fluorodopa">F-DOPA</a> '
             'uptake in the <a href="Striatum" title="Striatum">striatum</a> of a PD patient, '
             'captured through <a href="Positron_emission_tomography" title="Positron emission '
             'tomography">PET</a></figcaption></figure> <p>Diagnosis can be aided by molecular '
             'imaging techniques such as <a href="Magnetic_resonance_imaging" title="Magnetic '
             'resonance imaging">magnetic resonance imaging</a> (MRI), <a '
             'href="Positron_emission_tomography" title="Positron emission tomography">positron '
             'emission tomography</a> (PET), and <a '
             'href="Single-photon_emission_computed_tomography" title="Single-photon emission '
             'computed tomography">single-photon emission computed tomography</a> (SPECT). As both '
             'conventional MRI and <a href="CT_scan" title="CT scan">computed tomography</a> (CT) '
             'scans are usually normal in early PD, they can be used to exclude other pathologies '
             'that cause parkinsonism. <a href="Diffusion-weighted_magnetic_resonance_imaging" '
             'title="Diffusion-weighted magnetic resonance imaging">Diffusion MRI</a> can '
             'differentiate PD from <a href="Multiple_system_atrophy" title="Multiple system '
             'atrophy">multiple system atrophy</a>. Emerging MRI techniques of at least 3.0 T <a '
             'href="Magnetic_resonance_imaging#Construction_and_physics" title="Magnetic resonance '
             'imaging">field strength</a>—including <a class="mw-redirect" href="MRI_sequence" '
             'title="MRI sequence">neuromelanin-MRI</a>, <a '
             'href="Magnetic_resonance_spectroscopic_imaging" title="Magnetic resonance '
             'spectroscopic imaging">1H-MRSI</a>, and resting state fMRI—may detect abnormalities '
             'in the substantia nigra, nigrostriatal pathway, and elsewhere. </p><p>Unlike MRI, '
             'PET and SPECT use radioisotopes for imaging. Both techniques can aid diagnosis by '
             'characterizing PD-associated alterations in the metabolism and <a '
             'href="Dopamine_transporter" title="Dopamine transporter">transport</a> of dopamine '
             'in the basal ganglia. Largely used outside the United States, '
             'iodine-123-meta-iodobenzylguanidine <a class="mw-redirect" href="Myocardium" '
             'title="Myocardium">myocardial</a> <a href="Scintigraphy" '
             'title="Scintigraphy">scintigraphy</a> can assess heart muscle denervation to support '
             'a PD diagnosis. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Differential_diagnosis">Differential diagnosis</h3></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">See also: <a href="Parkinson-plus_syndrome" '
             'title="Parkinson-plus syndrome">Parkinson-plus syndrome</a></div> <p><a '
             'href="Differential_diagnosis" title="Differential diagnosis">Differential '
             'diagnosis</a> of Parkinson\'s is among the most difficult in <a href="Neurology" '
             'title="Neurology">neurology</a>. Differentiating early PD from <a '
             'href="Parkinson-plus_syndrome" title="Parkinson-plus syndrome">atypical parkinsonian '
             'disorders</a> is a major difficulty. In their initial stages, PD can be difficult to '
             'distinguish from the atypical neurodegenerative parkinsonisms, including <a '
             'href="Multiple_system_atrophy" title="Multiple system atrophy">multiple system '
             'atrophy</a>, <a href="Dementia_with_Lewy_bodies" title="Dementia with Lewy '
             'bodies">dementia with Lewy bodies</a>, and the <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Tauopathies" title="Tauopathies">tauopathies</a> <a '
             'href="Progressive_supranuclear_palsy" title="Progressive supranuclear '
             'palsy">progressive supranuclear palsy</a> and <a href="Corticobasal_degeneration" '
             'title="Corticobasal degeneration">corticobasal degeneration</a>. Other conditions '
             'that may present similarly to PD include vascular Parkinsonism, <a '
             'href="Alzheimer\'s_disease" title="Alzheimer\'s disease">Alzheimer\'s disease</a>, '
             'and <a href="Frontotemporal_dementia" title="Frontotemporal dementia">frontotemporal '
             'dementia</a>. </p><p>The International Parkinson and Movement Disorder Society has '
             "proposed a set of criteria that, unlike the standard Queen's Square Brain Bank "
             'Criteria, includes non-exclusionary "red-flag" clinical features that may not '
             'suggest PD. A large number of "red flags" has been proposed and adopted for various '
             'conditions that might mimic the symptoms of PD. Diagnostic tests, including gene '
             'sequencing, molecular imaging techniques, and assessment of smell may also '
             'distinguish PD. MRI is particularly powerful due to several unique features for '
             'atypical parkinsonisms. </p> <table class="wikitable plainrowheaders"> <tbody><tr> '
             '<th scope="col">Disorder </th> <th scope="col">Distinguishing symptoms and features '
             '</th></tr> <tr> <th scope="row"><a href="Corticobasal_syndrome" title="Corticobasal '
             'syndrome">Corticobasal syndrome</a> </th> <td>Levodopa resistance, <a '
             'href="Myoclonus" title="Myoclonus">myoclonus</a>, <a href="Dystonia" '
             'title="Dystonia">dystonia</a>, corticosensory loss, <a href="Alien_hand_syndrome" '
             'title="Alien hand syndrome">alien limb phenomenon</a>, <a href="Apraxia" '
             'title="Apraxia">apraxia</a>, and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Non-fluent_aphasia" '
             'title="Non-fluent aphasia">non-fluent aphasia</a> </td></tr> <tr> <th scope="row"><a '
             'href="Dementia_with_Lewy_bodies" title="Dementia with Lewy bodies">Dementia with '
             'Lewy bodies</a> </th> <td>Levodopa resistance, cognitive predominance before motor '
             'symptoms, and fluctuating cognitive symptoms </td></tr> <tr> <th scope="row"><a '
             'href="Essential_tremor" title="Essential tremor">Essential tremor</a> </th> '
             '<td>Tremor that worsens with action, normal SPECT scan </td></tr> <tr> <th '
             'scope="row"><a href="Multiple_system_atrophy" title="Multiple system '
             'atrophy">Multiple system atrophy</a> </th> <td>Levodopa resistance, rapidly '
             'progressive, autonomic failure, stridor, present <a href="Plantar_reflex" '
             'title="Plantar reflex">Babinski sign</a>, cerebellar ataxia, and specific MRI '
             'findings like the "Hot Cross Bun" </td></tr> <tr> <th scope="row"><a '
             'href="Progressive_supranuclear_palsy" title="Progressive supranuclear '
             'palsy">Progressive supranuclear palsy</a> </th> <td>Levodopa resistance, restrictive '
             'vertical gaze, <a href="Pseudobulbar_affect" title="Pseudobulbar '
             'affect">pseudobulbar crying</a>, <a href="Blepharospasm" '
             'title="Blepharospasm">eyelid twitching</a>, specific MRI findings, and early and '
             'different postural difficulties </td></tr> </tbody></table> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Management">Management</h2></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Management_of_Parkinson\'s_disease" title="Management of Parkinson\'s '
             'disease">Management of Parkinson\'s disease</a></div> <p>As of 2025, no '
             'disease-modifying therapies exist that reverse or slow neurodegeneration. Management '
             'typically combines lifestyle modifications with <a href="Physical_therapy" '
             'title="Physical therapy">physical therapy</a>. Current pharmacological interventions '
             'purely target symptoms, by either increasing endogenous <a href="Dopamine" '
             'title="Dopamine">dopamine</a> levels or directly mimicking dopamine\'s effect on the '
             "patient's brain. These include dopamine agonists, MAO-B inhibitors, and levodopa—the "
             'most widely used and effective drug. The optimal time to initiate pharmacological '
             'treatment is debated, but initial dopamine agonist and MAO-B inhibitor treatment and '
             'later levodopa therapy are common. Invasive procedures such as <a '
             'href="Deep_brain_stimulation" title="Deep brain stimulation">deep brain '
             'stimulation</a> may be used when medications are ineffective. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Medications">Medications</h3></div> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Levodopa">Levodopa</h4></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="1468" data-file-width="2000" decoding="async" height="147" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Stalevo.jpg" width="200"/><figcaption>LCE (<a href="Levodopa" '
             'title="Levodopa">levodopa</a>/<a href="Carbidopa" title="Carbidopa">carbidopa</a>/<a '
             'href="Entacapone" title="Entacapone">entacapone</a>) pills contain a cocktail of the '
             'dopamine precursor levadopa and COMT and AAAD inhibitors.</figcaption></figure> '
             '<p><a href="Levodopa" title="Levodopa">Levodopa</a> is the most widely used and most '
             'effective therapy—the <a href="Gold_standard_(test)" title="Gold standard '
             '(test)">gold standard</a>—for PD treatment. The compound occurs naturally and is the '
             'immediate precursor for dopamine synthesis in the dopaminergic neurons of the '
             'substantia nigra. Levodopa administration reduces the dopamine deficiency in '
             'parkinsonism. </p><p>Despite its efficacy, levodopa poses several challenges. Its '
             'administration has been called the "pharmacologist\'s nightmare". Its metabolism '
             'outside the brain by <a href="Aromatic_L-amino_acid_decarboxylase" title="Aromatic '
             'L-amino acid decarboxylase">aromatic L-amino acid decarboxylase</a> and '
             'catechol-O-methyltransferase can cause nausea and vomiting; inhibitors such as <a '
             'href="Carbidopa" title="Carbidopa">carbidopa</a>, <a href="Entacapone" '
             'title="Entacapone">entacapone</a>, and <a href="Benserazide" '
             'title="Benserazide">benserazide</a> are usually taken with levodopa to mitigate '
             'these effects. Long-term levodopa use may also <a href="Levodopa-induced_dyskinesia" '
             'title="Levodopa-induced dyskinesia">induce dyskinesia</a> and motor fluctuations. '
             'Although this often causes levodopa use to be delayed to later stages, earlier '
             'administration leads to improved motor function and quality of life. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Dopamine_agonists">Dopamine '
             'agonists</h4></div> <p><a class="mw-redirect" href="Dopamine_agonists" '
             'title="Dopamine agonists">Dopamine agonists</a> are an alternative or complement to '
             'levodopa therapy. They activate dopamine receptors in the striatum, with reduced '
             'risk of motor fluctuations and dyskinesia, and are efficacious in both early- and '
             'late-stage PD. The agonist <a href="Apomorphine" title="Apomorphine">apomorphine</a> '
             'is often used for drug-resistant OFF time in later-stage PD. After five years of '
             'use, impulse-control disorders may occur in over 40% of those taking dopamine '
             'agonists. A problematic, narcotic-like withdrawal effect may occur when agonist use '
             'is reduced or stopped. Compared to levodopa, dopamine agonists are more likely to '
             'cause fatigue, daytime sleepiness, and hallucinations. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading4"><h4 id="MAO-B_inhibitors">MAO-B inhibitors</h4></div> <p>MAO-B '
             'inhibitors—such as <a href="Safinamide" title="Safinamide">safinamide</a>, <a '
             'href="Selegiline" title="Selegiline">selegiline</a> and <a href="Rasagiline" '
             'title="Rasagiline">rasagiline</a>—increase the amount of dopamine in the basal '
             'ganglia by inhibiting the activity of <a href="Monoamine_oxidase_B" title="Monoamine '
             'oxidase B">monoamine oxidase B</a>, an enzyme that breaks down dopamine. These '
             'compounds mildly alleviate motor symptoms when used as monotherapy, but can also be '
             'used with levodopa and at any disease stage. Common side effects are nausea, '
             'dizziness, insomnia, sleepiness, and orthostatic hypotension. MAO-Bs are known to '
             'increase serotonin and cause a potentially dangerous condition known as <a '
             'href="Serotonin_syndrome" title="Serotonin syndrome">serotonin syndrome</a>. </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Other_drugs">Other drugs</h4></div> '
             '<p>Treatments for non-motor symptoms of PD have not been well studied, and many '
             'medications are used <a class="mw-redirect" href="Off-label" '
             'title="Off-label">off-label</a>. A diverse range of symptoms beyond those related to '
             'motor function can be treated pharmaceutically. Examples include <a '
             'href="Acetylcholinesterase_inhibitor" title="Acetylcholinesterase '
             'inhibitor">cholinesterase inhibitors</a> for cognitive impairment and <a '
             'href="Modafinil" title="Modafinil">modafinil</a> for <a '
             'href="Excessive_daytime_sleepiness" title="Excessive daytime sleepiness">excessive '
             'daytime sleepiness</a>. <a href="Fludrocortisone" '
             'title="Fludrocortisone">Fludrocortisone</a>, <a href="Midodrine" '
             'title="Midodrine">midodrine</a>, and <a href="Droxidopa" '
             'title="Droxidopa">droxidopa</a> are commonly used off-label for orthostatic '
             'hypotension related to autonomic dysfunction. Sublingual <a href="Atropine" '
             'title="Atropine">atropine</a> or <a href="Botulinum_toxin" title="Botulinum '
             'toxin">botulinum toxin</a> injections may be used off-label for drooling. <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="SSRIs" title="SSRIs">SSRIs</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="SNRIs" title="SNRIs">SNRIs</a> are often used for depression related to PD, '
             'but a risk exists of <a href="Serotonin_syndrome" title="Serotonin '
             'syndrome">serotonin syndrome</a> with the SSRI or SNRI antidepressants. Doxepin and '
             'rasagline may reduce physical fatigue in PD. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Invasive_interventions">Invasive interventions</h3></div> '
             '<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="1174" data-file-width="787" decoding="async" height="283" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Parkinson_surgery.jpg" width="190"/><figcaption>Placement of '
             'an electrode into the brain for <a href="Deep_brain_stimulation" title="Deep brain '
             'stimulation">deep brain stimulation</a></figcaption></figure> <p>Surgery for PD '
             'first appeared in the 19th century, and by the 1960s, had evolved into <a '
             'href="Ablative_brain_surgery" title="Ablative brain surgery">ablative brain '
             'surgery</a> that lesioned the <a href="Basal_ganglia" title="Basal ganglia">basal '
             'ganglia</a>, <a href="Thalamus" title="Thalamus">thalamus</a>, or <a '
             'href="Globus_pallidus" title="Globus pallidus">globus pallidus</a> (a <a '
             'href="Pallidotomy" title="Pallidotomy">pallidotomy</a>). The discovery of levadopa '
             'for PD treatment caused ablative therapies to largely disappear. Ablative surgeries '
             'experienced a resurgence in the 1990s, but were quickly superseded by newly '
             'developed <a href="Deep_brain_stimulation" title="Deep brain stimulation">deep brain '
             'stimulation</a> (DBS). Although <a class="mw-redirect" href="Gamma_knife" '
             'title="Gamma knife">gamma knife</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="High-intensity_focused_ultrasound" title="High-intensity focused '
             'ultrasound">high-intensity focused ultrasound</a> surgeries have been developed for '
             'pallidotomies and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Thalamotomies" '
             'title="Thalamotomies">thalamotomies</a>, their use is rare as of 2025. </p><p>DBS '
             'involves the implantation of electrodes called <a href="Neurostimulation" '
             'title="Neurostimulation">neurostimulators</a>, which send electrical impulses to '
             'specific parts of the brain. DBS for the <a href="Subthalamic_nucleus" '
             'title="Subthalamic nucleus">subthalamic nucleus</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Globus_pallidus_interna" title="Globus pallidus interna">globus pallidus '
             'interna</a> has high efficacy for up to 2 years, but long-term efficacy is unclear '
             'and likely decreases with time. DBS typically targets rigidity and tremor, and is '
             'recommended for PD patients who are intolerant or do not respond to medication. '
             'Cognitive impairment is the most common exclusion criteria. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Rehabilitation">Rehabilitation</h3></div> '
             '<div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Further information: <a '
             'href="Management_of_Parkinson\'s_disease#Rehabilitation" title="Management of '
             'Parkinson\'s disease">Management of Parkinson\'s disease §\xa0'
             'Rehabilitation</a></div> <p>Although pharmacological therapies can improve symptoms, '
             'autonomy, and the ability to perform everyday tasks is still reduced by PD. '
             'Rehabilitation is often useful, but the scientific support for any single '
             'rehabilitation treatment is limited. </p><p>Exercise programs are often recommended, '
             'with preliminary evidence of efficacy. Regular <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Physical_exercise" title="Physical exercise">physical exercise</a> with or '
             'without physical therapy can be beneficial to maintain and improve mobility, '
             'flexibility, strength, gait speed, and quality of life. Aerobic, mind-body, and '
             'resistance training may be beneficial in alleviating PD-associated depression and '
             'anxiety. <a href="Strength_training" title="Strength training">Strength training</a> '
             'may increase <a class="mw-redirect" href="Manual_dexterity" title="Manual '
             'dexterity">manual dexterity</a> and strength, facilitating daily tasks that require '
             'grasping objects. Aerobic exercise, resistance training, and balance- and '
             'task-specific training have been found to improve strength, <a href="VO2_max" '
             'title="VO2 max">VO Max</a> and balance. While flexibility training is commonly used, '
             'it has a lower strength of recommendation compared to aerobic and resistance '
             'training. </p> <figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" '
             'typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="A Parkinson\'s patient on a tricycle" '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="2448" data-file-width="3264" '
             'decoding="async" height="188" loading="lazy" src="./Three_Wheeler.jpg" '
             'width="250"/><figcaption>Exercise, like the tricycle ride of this PD patient, is '
             'often recommended.</figcaption></figure> <p>In improving flexibility and range of '
             'motion for people experiencing rigidity, generalized relaxation techniques such as '
             'gentle rocking have been found to decrease excessive muscle tension. Other effective '
             'techniques to promote relaxation include slow rotational movements of the '
             'extremities and trunk, rhythmic initiation, <a href="Diaphragmatic_breathing" '
             'title="Diaphragmatic breathing">diaphragmatic breathing</a>, and <a '
             'href="Meditation" title="Meditation">meditation</a>. Deep diaphragmatic breathing '
             'may also improve chest-wall mobility and <a href="Vital_capacity" title="Vital '
             'capacity">vital capacity</a> decreased by the stooped posture and respiratory '
             "dysfunctions of advanced Parkinson's. Rehabilitation techniques targeting gait and "
             'the challenges posed by bradykinesia, shuffling, and decreased arm swing include '
             'pole walking, treadmill walking, and marching exercises. Long-term physiotherapy '
             '(greater than six months) reduces the need for antiparkinsonian medication; '
             'multidisciplinary rehabilitation programs combined with physiotherapy can result in '
             'reduction in the levodopa-equivalent dose. </p><p><a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Speech_therapies" title="Speech therapies">Speech therapies</a> such as the <a '
             'href="Lee_Silverman_voice_treatment" title="Lee Silverman voice treatment">Lee '
             'Silverman voice treatment</a> may reduce the effect of speech disorders associated '
             'with PD. <a href="Occupational_therapy" title="Occupational therapy">Occupational '
             'therapy</a> as a rehabilitation strategy can improve quality of life by enabling '
             'people with PD to find engaging activities and communal roles, adapt to their living '
             'environment, and improve domestic and work abilities. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Diet">Diet</h3></div> <p>PD poses digestive problems such as '
             'constipation and <a href="Gastroparesis" title="Gastroparesis">prolonged emptying of '
             'stomach contents</a>, and a balanced diet with periodical nutritional assessments is '
             'recommended to avoid weight loss or gain and minimize the consequences of '
             'gastrointestinal dysfunction. In particular, a Mediterranean diet is advised and may '
             'slow disease progression. As it can compete for uptake with <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Amino_acids" title="Amino acids">amino acids</a> derived from protein, '
             'levodopa should be taken 30 minutes before meals to minimize such competition. '
             'Low-protein diets may also be needed by later stages. As the disease advances, '
             'swallowing difficulties often arise. Using thickening agents for liquid intake and '
             'an upright posture when eating may be useful; both measures reduce the risk of '
             'choking. <a href="Gastrostomy" title="Gastrostomy">Gastrostomy</a> can be used to '
             'deliver food directly into the stomach. Increased water and fiber intakes are used '
             'to treat constipation. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Palliative_care">Palliative care</h3></div> <p>As PD is incurable, palliative '
             'care aims to improve the quality of life for both the patient and family by '
             'alleviating the symptoms and stress associated with illness. Early integration of '
             'palliative care into the disease course is recommended, rather than delaying until '
             'later stages. Palliative-care specialists can help with physical symptoms, emotional '
             'factors such as loss of function and jobs, depression, and fear, as well as '
             'existential concerns. Palliative-care team members also help guide difficult '
             'decisions caused by disease progression, such as wishes for a <a href="Feeding_tube" '
             'title="Feeding tube">feeding tube</a>, <a href="Non-invasive_ventilation" '
             'title="Non-invasive ventilation">noninvasive ventilator</a> or <a href="Tracheotomy" '
             'title="Tracheotomy">tracheostomy</a>, use of <a href="Cardiopulmonary_resuscitation" '
             'title="Cardiopulmonary resuscitation">cardiopulmonary resuscitation</a>, and '
             'entering <a href="Hospice" title="Hospice">hospice</a> care. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Prognosis">Prognosis</h2></div> <div '
             'class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">See also: <a '
             'href="Unified_Parkinson\'s_disease_rating_scale" title="Unified Parkinson\'s disease '
             'rating scale">Unified Parkinson\'s disease rating scale</a></div> <table '
             'class="wikitable" style="float:right; margin-left:1em; font-size:90%; '
             'line-height:1.4em; width:350px;"> <caption>Prognosis of PD subtypes </caption> '
             '<tbody><tr> <th rowspan="2" '
             'style="background:#011E41;color:white;text-align:center;">Parkinson\'s subtype </th> '
             '<th colspan="2" style="background:#011E41;color:white;text-align:center;">Mean years '
             'post-diagnosis until: </th></tr> <tr> <th '
             'style="background:#011E41;color:white;">Severe cognitive or movement abnormalities '
             '</th> <th style="background:#011E41;color:white;">Death </th></tr> <tr> '
             '<th>Mild-motor predominant </th> <td style="text-align:center;">14.3 </td> <td '
             'style="text-align:center;">20.2 </td></tr> <tr> <th>Intermediate </th> <td '
             'style="text-align:center;">8.2 </td> <td style="text-align:center;">13.1 </td></tr> '
             '<tr> <th>Diffuse malignant </th> <td style="text-align:center;">3.5 </td> <td '
             'style="text-align:center;">8.1 </td></tr> </tbody></table> <p>As PD is a <a '
             'href="Heterogeneous_condition" title="Heterogeneous condition">heterogeneous '
             'condition</a> with multiple <a href="Cause_(medicine)" title="Cause '
             '(medicine)">etiologies</a>, prognostication can be difficult and prognoses can be '
             'highly variable. On average, life expectancy is reduced in those with PD, with '
             'younger age of onset resulting in greater life expectancy decreases. Although PD '
             "subtype categorization is controversial, the 2017 Parkinson's Progression Markers "
             'Initiative study identified three broad scorable subtypes of increasing severity and '
             'more rapid progression - mild-motor predominant, intermediate, and diffuse '
             'malignant. Mean years of survival after diagnosis were 20.2, 13.1, and 8.1. '
             '</p><p>Around 30% of individuals with PD develop dementia, which is 12 times more '
             'likely to occur in the elderly with severe PD. Dementia is less likely to arise in '
             "tremor-dominant PD. Parkinson's disease dementia is associated with a reduced <a "
             'href="Quality_of_life" title="Quality of life">quality of life</a> in people with PD '
             'and their <a href="Caregiver" title="Caregiver">caregivers</a>, increased mortality, '
             'and a higher probability of needing <a class="mw-redirect" href="Nursing_home_care" '
