             'title="Nursing home care">nursing home care</a>. </p><p>The incidence rate of falls '
             'is around 45 to 68%, three times that of healthy individuals, and half of such falls '
             'result in serious secondary injuries. Falls increase <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Morbidity" title="Morbidity">morbidity</a> and <a href="Mortality_rate" '
             'title="Mortality rate">mortality</a>. Around 90% of those with PD develop <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Hypokinetic_dysarthria" title="Hypokinetic '
             'dysarthria">hypokinetic dysarthria</a>, which worsens with disease progression and '
             'can hinder communication. Over 80% develop dysphagia; consequent inhalation of '
             'gastric and oropharyngeal secretions can lead to <a href="Aspiration_pneumonia" '
             'title="Aspiration pneumonia">aspiration pneumonia</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Epidemiology">Epidemiology</h2></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="640" data-file-width="433" decoding="async" height="281" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Cropduster_spraying_pesticides.jpg" '
             'width="190"/><figcaption>Agricultural areas are associated with higher PD <a '
             'href="Prevalence" title="Prevalence">prevalence</a>, possibly due to exposure to '
             'pesticides and industrial waste.</figcaption></figure> <p>As of 2024, PD is the '
             'second-most common neurodegenerative disease and the fastest growing in total cases. '
             'As of 2023, global <a href="Prevalence" title="Prevalence">prevalence</a> was '
             'estimated to be 1.51 per 1000. Although it is around 40% more common in men, age is '
             'the dominant predeterminant of PD. Consequently, as global life expectancy has '
             'increased, PD prevalence has also risen, with an estimated increase in cases by 74% '
             'from 1990 to 2016. The number is predicted to rise to over 12 million by 2040. '
             '</p><p>This increase may be due to a number of global factors, including prolonged '
             'life expectancy, increased industrialisation, and decreased smoking. Although '
             'genetics is the sole factor in a minority of cases, most cases of PD are likely a '
             'result of <a class="mw-redirect" href="Gene-environment_interactions" '
             'title="Gene-environment interactions">gene-environment interactions</a>; concordance '
             'studies with <a class="mw-redirect" href="Twins" title="Twins">twins</a> have found '
             'PD <a href="Heritability" title="Heritability">heritability</a> to be just 30%. The '
             'influence of multiple genetic and environmental factors complicates epidemiological '
             'efforts. </p><p>Relative to Europe and North America, disease prevalence is lower in '
             'Africa, but similar in Latin America. Although China is predicted to have nearly '
             'half of the global PD population by 2030, estimates of prevalence in Asia vary. '
             'Potential explanations for these geographic differences include genetic variation, '
             'environmental factors, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Health_care_access" '
             'title="Health care access">health care access</a>, and life expectancy. Although PD '
             'incidence and prevalence may vary by race and ethnicity, significant disparities in '
             'care, diagnosis, and study participation limit generalizability and lead to '
             'conflicting results. Within the United States, high rates of PD have been identified '
             'in the Midwest, the South, and agricultural regions of other states, collectively '
             'termed the "PD belt". The association between rural residence and PD has been '
             'hypothesized to be caused by environmental factors including herbicides, pesticides, '
             'and industrial waste. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="History">History</h2></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" '
             'role="note">Main article: <a href="History_of_Parkinson\'s_disease" title="History '
             'of Parkinson\'s disease">History of Parkinson\'s disease</a></div> <div class="thumb '
             'tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" '
             'style="width:392px;max-width:392px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" '
             'style="width:171px;max-width:171px"><div class="thumbimage" '
             'style="height:223px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="A black and '
             'white portrait photograph of Jean-Martin Charcot" class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="604" data-file-width="457" decoding="async" height="223" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Jean-Martin_Charcot.jpg" width="169"/></span></div></div><div '
             'class="tsingle" style="width:217px;max-width:217px"><div class="thumbimage" '
             'style="height:223px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="Side and front '
             'views of a Parkinson\'s patient, illustrating hunched posture" '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="654" data-file-width="630" '
             'decoding="async" height="223" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Photographs_of_a_Parkinson_patient_Pierre_D.jpg" '
             'width="215"/></span></div></div></div><div class="trow" style="display:flex"><div '
             'class="thumbcaption">In 1877, Jean-Martin Charcot (left) named Parkinson\'s disease '
             'after the first person to comprehensively describe it, James Parkinson. Patient '
             "Pierre D. (right) served as the model for William Gowers' illustration of the "
             'disease.</div></div></div></div> <p>In 1817, English physician James Parkinson '
             'published the first full medical description of the disease as a neurological '
             'syndrome in his monograph <i>An Essay on the Shaking Palsy</i>. He presented six '
             'clinical cases, including three he had observed on the streets near Hoxton Square in '
             'London. Parkinson described three cardinal symptoms: tremor, postural instability, '
             'and "paralysis" (undistinguished from rigidity or bradykinesia), and speculated that '
             'the disease was caused by trauma to the <a href="Spinal_cord" title="Spinal '
             'cord">spinal cord</a>. </p><p>Little discussion of or investigation into the '
             '"shaking palsy" occurred until 1861, when Frenchman Jean-Martin Charcot—regarded as '
             'the father of <a href="Neurology" title="Neurology">neurology</a>—began expanding '
             "Parkinson's description, adding bradykinesia as one of the four cardinal symptoms. "
             'In 1877, Charcot renamed the disease after Parkinson, as the tremor suggested by '
             '"shaking palsy" is not present in all. Subsequent neurologists who made early '
             'advances to the understanding of PD include Armand Trousseau, William Gowers, Samuel '
             'Kinnier Wilson, and Wilhelm Erb. </p><p>Although Parkinson is typically credited '
             'with the first detailed description of PD, many previous texts reference some of the '
             "disease's clinical signs. In his essay, Parkinson himself acknowledged partial "
             'descriptions by Galen, William Cullen, Johann Juncker, and others. Possible earlier '
             'but incomplete descriptions include a Nineteenth Dynasty Egyptian papyrus, the <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Ayurvedic" title="Ayurvedic">ayurvedic</a> text <i>Charaka '
             'Samhita</i>, Ecclesiastes 12:3, and a discussion of tremors by Leonardo da Vinci. '
             'Multiple <a href="Traditional_Chinese_medicine" title="Traditional Chinese '
             'medicine">traditional Chinese medicine</a> texts may include references to PD, '
             "including a discussion in the Yellow Emperor's Internal Classic (<abbr "
             'title="circa">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;">\u2009425–221 BC</span>) of '
             'a disease with symptoms of tremor, stiffness, staring, and stooped posture. In 2009, '
             'a systematic description of PD was found in the Hungarian medical text <i>Pax '
             'corporis</i> written by Ferenc Pápai Páriz in 1690, some 120 years before Parkinson. '
             'Although Páriz correctly described all four cardinal signs, it was only published in '
             'Hungarian and was not widely distributed. </p><p>In 1912, Frederic Lewy described '
             'microscopic particles in affected brains, later named Lewy bodies. In 1919, '
             'Konstantin Tretiakoff reported that the substantia nigra was the main brain '
             'structure affected, corroborated by Rolf Hassler in 1938. The underlying changes in '
             'dopamine signaling were identified in the 1950s, largely by Arvid Carlsson and Oleh '
             'Hornykiewicz. In 1997, Polymeropoulos and colleagues at the <a '
             'href="National_Institutes_of_Health" title="National Institutes of Health">NIH</a> '
             'discovered the first gene for PD, <i>SNCA</i>, which encodes alpha-synuclein. '
             'Alpha-synuclein was, in turn, found to be the main component of Lewy bodies by '
             'Spillantini, Trojanowski, Goedert, and others. Anticholinergics and surgery were the '
             'only treatments until the use of levodopa, which, although first synthesized by '
             'Casimir Funk in 1911, did not enter clinical use until 1967. By the late 1980s, deep '
             'brain stimulation introduced by Alim Louis Benabid and colleagues at Grenoble, '
             'France, emerged as an additional treatment. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Society_and_culture">Society and culture</h2></div> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Social_impact">Social impact</h3></div> '
             '<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="A sketch showing '
             'hypomimia, a blank, expressionless face" class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="1123" data-file-width="708" decoding="async" height="286" '
             'loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Drawing_of_face_of_parkinsons_disease_patient_showing_hypomimia.jpg" '
             'width="180"/><figcaption>The <a href="Hypomimia" title="Hypomimia">reduced ability '
             'to facially express emotions</a>—as depicted here by French anatomist Paul Richer in '
             '1888—can harm social well-being.</figcaption></figure> <p>For some people with PD, '
             'masked facial expressions and difficulty moderating facial expressions of emotion or '
             "recognizing other people's facial expressions can impact social well-being. As the "
             'condition progresses, tremor, other motor symptoms, difficulty communicating, or '
             'mobility issues may interfere with social engagement, causing individuals with PD to '
             'feel isolated. Public perception and awareness of PD symptoms such as shaking, '
             'hallucinating, slurring speech, and being off balance is lacking in some countries '
             'and can lead to stigma. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Cost">Cost</h3></div> <p>The economic cost of Parkinson\'s to both individuals '
             'and society is high. In many low- and middle-income countries, public health systems '
             "may not fully cover Parkinson's disease therapies, leading to disparities in access "
             'to treatment. In contrast, high-income countries with universal healthcare typically '
             'cover standard treatments such as levodopa and specialist care. Indirect costs '
             'include lifetime earnings losses due to premature death, productivity losses, and '
             'caregiver burdens. The duration and progressive nature of PD can place a heavy '
             'burden on caregivers; family members, such as spouses, dedicate around 22 hours per '
             'week to care. </p><p>In 2010, the total economic burden of PD across Europe, '
             'including indirect and direct medical costs, was estimated to be €13.9 billion (US '
             '$14.9 billion). The total burden in the United States was estimated to be $51.9\xa0'
             'billion in 2017, and is projected to surpass $79\xa0billion by 2037. As of 2022, no '
             'rigorous economic surveys had been performed for low- or middle-income nations. '
             'Preventive care has been identified as crucial to slow the rapidly increasing '
             "incidence of Parkinson's from overwhelming national health systems. </p> <div "
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Advocacy">Advocacy</h3></div> <p>The birthday '
             "of James Parkinson, 11 April, has been designated as World Parkinson's Day. A red "
             'tulip was chosen by international organizations as the symbol of the disease in '
             "2005; it represents the 'James Parkinson' tulip cultivar, registered in 1981 by a "
             'Dutch horticulturalist. </p><p>Advocacy organizations include the <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="National_Parkinson_Foundation" title="National Parkinson '
             'Foundation">National Parkinson Foundation</a>, which has provided more than $180\xa0'
             'million in care, research, and support services since 1982, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Parkinson\'s_Disease_Foundation" title="Parkinson\'s Disease '
             'Foundation">Parkinson\'s Disease Foundation</a>, which has distributed more than '
             '$115\xa0million for research and nearly $50\xa0million for education and advocacy '
             'programs since its founding in 1957 by William Black; the <a '
             'href="American_Parkinson_Disease_Association" title="American Parkinson Disease '
             'Association">American Parkinson Disease Association</a>, founded in 1961; and the '
             "European Parkinson's Disease Association, founded in 1992. </p> <div "
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Notable_cases">Notable cases</h3></div> '
             '<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="Michael J. Fox and '
             'Muhammad Ali are seen speaking to reporters, with the marble walls of the U.S. '
             'Senate behind them" class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="884" '
             'data-file-width="1182" decoding="async" height="187" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./0522_ma_big_cropped.png" width="250"/><figcaption>Actor Michael J. Fox and '
             'boxer Muhammad Ali (center) are pictured in 2002 speaking before the US Senate to '
             "urge increased funding for Parkinson's research.</figcaption></figure> <p>In the "
             "21st century, the diagnosis of PD among notable figures has increased the public's "
             'understanding of the disorder. Actor Michael J. Fox was diagnosed with PD at 29 '
             'years old, and has used his diagnosis to increase awareness of the disease. To '
             'illustrate the effects of the disease, Fox has appeared without medication in '
             'television roles and before the United States Congress. The Michael J. Fox '
             "Foundation, which he founded in 2000, has raised over $2 billion for Parkinson's "
             'research. </p><p>Boxer Muhammad Ali showed signs of PD when he was 38, but was '
             'undiagnosed until he was 42; he has been called the "world\'s most famous '
             'Parkinson\'s patient".  Whether he had PD or <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Dementia_pugilistica" title="Dementia pugilistica">parkinsonism related to '
             'boxing</a> is unresolved. Cyclist and Olympic medalist Davis Phinney, diagnosed with '
             'PD at 40, started the <a href="Davis_Phinney_Foundation" title="Davis Phinney '
             'Foundation">Davis Phinney Foundation</a> in 2004 to support PD research. '
             '</p><p>Musician Ozzy Osbourne was diagnosed with PD; he performed a July 5, 2025, '
             'farewell reunion concert with the band he co-founded, Black Sabbath, weeks before '
             "his death. The event raised over $190 million, some of which went to Parkinson's "
             'disease research. </p><p>Adolf Hitler is believed to have had PD, and the condition '
             'may have influenced his decision-making. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Clinical_research">Clinical research</h2></div> <div '
             'class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Research_in_Parkinson\'s_disease" title="Research in Parkinson\'s '
             'disease">Research in Parkinson\'s disease</a></div> <figure class="mw-default-size" '
             'typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="A European astronaut is seen in zero gravity '
             'conducting research with a micropipette" class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="3712" data-file-width="5568" decoding="async" height="167" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./ISS-57_Alexander_Gerst_works_in_the_Harmony_module_(1).jpg" '
             'width="250"/><figcaption>Astronaut Alexander Gerst conducted PD research aboard the '
             'International Space Station in 2018.</figcaption></figure> <p>As of 2024, no '
             'disease-modifying therapies exist that reverse or slow the progression of PD. Active '
             'research directions include the search for new <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Animal_model" title="Animal model">animal models</a> of the disease and '
             'development and trial of <a href="Gene_therapy" title="Gene therapy">gene '
             'therapy</a>, <a href="Stem_cell" title="Stem cell">stem cell</a> transplants, and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Neuroprotective" '
             'title="Neuroprotective">neuroprotective</a> agents. Improved treatments will likely '
             'combine therapeutic strategies to manage symptoms and enhance outcomes. Reliable <a '
             'href="Biomarker_(medicine)" title="Biomarker (medicine)">biomarkers</a> are needed '
             'for early diagnosis, and research criteria for their identification have been '
             'established. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Neuroprotective_treatments">Neuroprotective treatments</h3></div> <div '
             'class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">See also: <a '
             'href="Anti-%CE%B1-synuclein_drug" title="Anti-α-synuclein drug">Anti-α-synuclein '
             'drug</a></div> <p><a class="mw-redirect" href="Anti-alpha-synuclein_drugs" '
             'title="Anti-alpha-synuclein drugs">Drugs that prevent alpha-synuclein</a> '
             'oligomerization and aggregation or promote their clearance are under active '
             'investigation, and potential therapeutic strategies include <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Small_molecules" title="Small molecules">small molecules</a> and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Immunotherapies" '
             'title="Immunotherapies">immunotherapies</a> including <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Vaccines" title="Vaccines">vaccines</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Monoclonal_antibodies" title="Monoclonal antibodies">monoclonal '
             'antibodies</a>. While immunotherapies show promise, their efficacy is often '
             'inconsistent. Anti-inflammatory drugs that target <a href="NLRP3" '
             'title="NLRP3">NLRP3</a> and the <a href="JAK-STAT_signaling_pathway" title="JAK-STAT '
             'signaling pathway">JAK-STAT signaling pathway</a> offer another potential '
             'therapeutic approach. </p><p>As the <a class="mw-redirect" href="Gut_microbiome" '
             'title="Gut microbiome">gut microbiome</a> in PD is often disrupted and produces '
             'toxic compounds, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Fecal_microbiota_transplants" '
             'title="Fecal microbiota transplants">fecal microbiota transplants</a> might restore '
             'a healthy microbiome and alleviate various motor and non-motor symptoms. <a '
             'href="Neurotrophic_factors" title="Neurotrophic factors">Neurotrophic factors</a>—<a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Peptides" title="Peptides">peptides</a> that enhance the '
             'growth, maturation, and survival of neurons—show modest results, but require '
             'invasive surgical administration. <a class="mw-redirect" href="Viral_vectors" '
             'title="Viral vectors">Viral vectors</a> may represent a more feasible delivery '
             'platform. Calcium channel blockers may restore the calcium imbalance present in PD, '
             'and are being investigated as a neuroprotective treatment. Other therapies, such as '
             '<a href="Deferiprone" title="Deferiprone">deferiprone</a>, may reduce the abnormal '
             'accumulation of iron in PD. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Cell-based_therapies">Cell-based therapies</h3></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Cell-based_therapies_for_Parkinson\'s_disease" title="Cell-based therapies for '
             'Parkinson\'s disease">Cell-based therapies for Parkinson\'s disease</a></div> <div '
             'class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" '
             'style="width:392px;max-width:392px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" '
             'style="width:166px;max-width:166px"><div class="thumbimage" '
             'style="height:165px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="Researchers at '
             'Argonne National Laboratory examining a culture of induced pluripotent stem cells" '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="1813" data-file-width="1800" '
             'decoding="async" height="165" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Parkinson\'s_induced_pluripotent_stem_cell.jpg" '
             'width="164"/></span></div></div><div class="tsingle" '
             'style="width:222px;max-width:222px"><div class="thumbimage" '
             'style="height:165px;overflow:hidden"><span '
             'typeof="mw:File"><span></span></span></div></div></div><div class="trow" '
             'style="display:flex"><div class="thumbcaption">Researchers at Argonne National '
             'Laboratory examine <a class="mw-redirect" href="Induced_pluripotent_stem_cells" '
             'title="Induced pluripotent stem cells">induced pluripotent stem cells</a> (iPSCs) '
             'for use in Parkinson\'s and other diseases: the <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Action_potentials" title="Action potentials">action potentials</a> of one such '
             'iPSC differentiated into a dopaminergic neuron are visible at '
             'right.</div></div></div></div> <p>In contrast to other neurodegenerative disorders, '
             'many PD symptoms can be attributed to the loss of a single cell type. Consequently, '
             'dopaminergic neuron regeneration is a promising therapeutic approach. Although most '
             'initial research sought to generate dopaminergic neuron precursor cells from fetal '
             'brain tissue, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Pluripotency" '
             'title="Pluripotency">pluripotent stem cells</a>—particularly <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Induced_pluripotent_stem_cells" title="Induced pluripotent stem cells">induced '
             'pluripotent stem cells</a> (iPSC)—have become an increasingly popular tissue source. '
             '</p><p>Both fetal and iPSC-derived DA neurons have been transplanted into patients '
             'in clinical trials. Although some individuals have seen improvement, the results are '
             'highly variable. Adverse effects, such as <a href="Tardive_dyskinesia" '
             'title="Tardive dyskinesia">dyskinesia</a> arising from excess dopamine release by '
             'the transplanted tissues, have also been observed. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Gene_therapy">Gene therapy</h3></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Gene_therapy_in_Parkinson\'s_disease" title="Gene therapy in Parkinson\'s '
             'disease">Gene therapy in Parkinson\'s disease</a></div> <p><a href="Gene_therapy" '
             'title="Gene therapy">Gene therapy</a> for PD seeks to restore the healthy function '
             'of dopaminergic neurons in the substantia nigra by delivering genetic '
             'material—typically through a viral vector—to these diseased cells. This material may '
             'deliver a functional, wild-type version of a gene, or knock down a pathological '
             'variant. Experimental gene therapies for PD have aimed to increase the expression of '
             'growth factors or enzymes involved in dopamine synthesis, such as <a '
             'href="Tyrosine_hydroxylase" title="Tyrosine hydroxylase">tyrosine hydroxylase</a>. '
             'The one-time delivery of genes circumvents the recurrent invasive administration '
             'required to administer some peptides and proteins to the brain. MicroRNAs are an '
             'emerging PD gene therapy platform that may serve as an alternative to viral vectors. '
             '</p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="See_also">See also</h2></div> '
             '<ul><li><a class="mw-redirect" href="NLRP3_inflammasome" title="NLRP3 '
             'inflammasome">NLRP3 inflammasome</a></li> <li><a href="Neuroinflammation" '

             'class="mw-page-container"> <div class="mw-page-container-inner"> <div '
             'class="mw-content-container"> <main class="mw-body" id="content"> <header '
             'class="mw-body-header vector-page-titlebar"> <h1 class="firstHeading '
             'mw-first-heading" id="firstHeading"> <span class="mw-page-title-main" '
             'id="openzim-page-title"><span class="mw-page-title-main">Dementia with Lewy '
             'bodies</span></span> </h1> </header> <a id="top"></a> <div '
             'aria-labelledby="firstHeading" class="vector-body '
             've-init-mw-desktopArticleTarget-targetContainer" data-mw-ve-target-container="" '
             'id="bodyContent"> <div class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" dir="ltr" '
             'id="mw-content-text" lang="en"><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" '
             'dir="ltr" lang="en"> <p class="mw-empty-elt"> </p> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Not to be confused with <a '
             'href="Lewy_body_dementia" title="Lewy body dementia">Lewy body dementia</a>, an '
             'umbrella term encompassing <a href="Parkinson\'s_disease_dementia" '
             'title="Parkinson\'s disease dementia">Parkinson\'s disease dementia</a> and dementia '
             'with Lewy bodies.</div> <p class="mw-empty-elt"> </p> <table class="infobox '
             'infobox-has-images-with-white-backgrounds"><tbody><tr><th class="infobox-above" '
             'colspan="2" style="background:#ccc">Dementia with Lewy bodies</th></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">Other names</th><td class="infobox-data">Diffuse '
             'Lewy body disease, dementia due to Lewy body disease</td></tr><tr><td '
             'class="infobox-full-data" colspan="2"><span class="mw-default-size" '
             'typeof="mw:File/Frameless"><img alt="See caption." class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="584" data-file-width="668" decoding="async" height="219" '
             'loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Lewy_body_in_the_substantia_nigra_from_a_person_with_Parkinson\'s_disease.jpg" '
             'width="250"/></span></td></tr><tr><td class="infobox-full-data" colspan="2"><a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Photomicrograph" title="Photomicrograph">Microscopic '
             'image</a> of a <a href="Lewy_body" title="Lewy body">Lewy body</a> (adjacent to '
             'arrowhead) in a neuron of the <a href="Substantia_nigra" title="Substantia '
             'nigra">substantia nigra</a>; scale bar=20 microns (0.02\xa0mm)</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row"><a href="Medical_specialty" title="Medical '
             'specialty">Specialty</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="Neurology" '
             'title="Neurology">Neurology</a>, <a href="Psychiatry" '
             'title="Psychiatry">psychiatry</a></td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row"><a href="Signs_and_symptoms" title="Signs and '
             'symptoms">Symptoms</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="Dementia" '
             'title="Dementia">Dementia</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="REM_sleep_behavior_disorder" title="REM sleep behavior disorder">abnormal '
             'behavior during REM sleep</a>, fluctuations in alertness, <a '
             'href="Hallucination#Visual" title="Hallucination">visual hallucinations</a>, <a '
             'href="Parkinsonism" title="Parkinsonism">parkinsonism</a></td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">Usual onset</th><td class="infobox-data">After the '
             'age of 50, median 76</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">Duration</th><td class="infobox-data">Long term</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row"><a href="Cause_(medicine)" title="Cause '
             '(medicine)">Causes</a></th><td class="infobox-data">Unknown</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row"><a href="Medical_diagnosis" title="Medical '
             'diagnosis">Diagnostic method</a></th><td class="infobox-data">Based on symptoms and '
             '<a href="Biomarker" title="Biomarker">biomarkers</a></td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row"><a href="Differential_diagnosis" '
             'title="Differential diagnosis">Differential diagnosis</a></th><td '
             'class="infobox-data"><a href="Alzheimer\'s_disease" title="Alzheimer\'s '
             'disease">Alzheimer\'s disease</a>, <a href="Parkinson\'s_disease_dementia" '
             'title="Parkinson\'s disease dementia">Parkinson\'s disease dementia</a>, certain <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Mental_illness" title="Mental illness">mental '
             'illnesses</a>, <a href="Vascular_dementia" title="Vascular dementia">vascular '
             'dementia</a></td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row"><a href="Medication" '
             'title="Medication">Medication</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="Donepezil" '
             'title="Donepezil">Donepezil</a>, <a href="Rivastigmine" '
             'title="Rivastigmine">rivastigmine</a> and <a href="Memantine" '
             'title="Memantine">memantine</a>; <a href="Melatonin" '
             'title="Melatonin">melatonin</a></td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row"><a href="Prognosis" title="Prognosis">Prognosis</a></th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Variable; average survival 4 years from '
             'diagnosis</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row">Frequency</th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">About 0.4% of persons older than 65</td></tr></tbody></table> '
             '<p><b>Dementia with Lewy bodies</b> (<b>DLB</b>) is a type of <a href="Dementia" '
             'title="Dementia">dementia</a> characterized by changes in sleep, <a '
             'href="Behavior_change_(individual)" title="Behavior change '
             '(individual)">behavior</a>, cognition, movement, and <a href="Dysautonomia" '
             'title="Dysautonomia">regulation of automatic bodily functions</a>. Unlike some other '
             'dementias, memory loss may not be an early symptom. The disease <a '
             'href="Progressive_disease" title="Progressive disease">worsens over time</a> and is '
             'usually diagnosed when cognitive impairment interferes with <a '
             'href="Activities_of_daily_living" title="Activities of daily living">normal daily '
             'functioning</a>. Together with <a href="Parkinson\'s_disease_dementia" '
             'title="Parkinson\'s disease dementia">Parkinson\'s disease dementia</a>, DLB is one '
             'of the two <a href="Lewy_body_dementia" title="Lewy body dementia">Lewy body '
             'dementias</a>. It is a common form of dementia, but the <a href="Prevalence" '
             'title="Prevalence">prevalence</a> is not known accurately and many diagnoses are '
             'missed. The disease was first described on autopsy by Kenji Kosaka in 1976, and he '
             'named the condition several years later. </p><p><a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="REM_sleep_behavior_disorder" title="REM sleep behavior disorder">REM sleep '
             'behavior disorder</a> (RBD)—in which people lose the muscle paralysis (atonia) that '
             'normally occurs during <a href="Rapid_eye_movement_sleep" title="Rapid eye movement '
             'sleep">REM sleep</a> and act out their dreams—is a core feature. RBD may appear '
             'years or decades before other symptoms. Other core features are <a '
             'href="Hallucination#Visual" title="Hallucination">visual hallucinations</a>, marked '
             'fluctuations in attention or alertness, and <a href="Parkinsonism" '
             'title="Parkinsonism">parkinsonism</a> (<a class="mw-redirect" href="Bradykinesia" '
             'title="Bradykinesia">slowness of movement</a>, trouble walking, or <a '
             'href="Hypokinesia#Cogwheel_rigidity" title="Hypokinesia">rigidity</a>). A '
             'presumptive diagnosis can be made if several disease features or <a href="Biomarker" '
             'title="Biomarker">biomarkers</a> are present; the diagnostic workup may include <a '
             'href="Blood_test" title="Blood test">blood tests</a>, <a '
             'href="Neuropsychological_test" title="Neuropsychological test">neuropsychological '
             'tests</a>, <a href="Medical_imaging" title="Medical imaging">imaging</a>, and <a '
             'href="Polysomnography" title="Polysomnography">sleep studies</a>. A definitive '
             'diagnosis usually requires an <a href="Autopsy" title="Autopsy">autopsy</a>.  '
             '</p><p>Most people with DLB do not have affected family members, although '
             'occasionally DLB runs in a family. The exact cause is unknown but involves formation '
             'of abnormal clumps of protein in <a href="Neuron" title="Neuron">neurons</a> '
             'throughout the brain. Manifesting as <a href="Lewy_body" title="Lewy body">Lewy '
             'bodies</a> (discovered in 1912 by Frederic Lewy) and <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Lewy_neurite" title="Lewy neurite">Lewy neurites</a>, these clumps affect both '
             'the <a href="Central_nervous_system" title="Central nervous system">central</a> and '
             'the <a href="Autonomic_nervous_system" title="Autonomic nervous '
             'system">autonomic</a> nervous systems. <a href="Cardiac_muscle" title="Cardiac '
             'muscle">Heart</a> function and every level of <a href="Gastrointestinal_tract" '
             'title="Gastrointestinal tract">gastrointestinal</a> function—from chewing to <a '
             'href="Defecation" title="Defecation">defecation</a>—can be affected, <a '
             'href="Constipation" title="Constipation">constipation</a> being one of the most '
             'common symptoms. <a href="Orthostatic_hypotension" title="Orthostatic '
             'hypotension">Low blood pressure upon standing</a> can also occur. DLB commonly '
             'causes psychiatric symptoms, such as altered behavior, <a href="Depression_(mood)" '
             'title="Depression (mood)">depression</a>, or apathy. </p><p>DLB typically begins '
             'after the age of fifty, and people with the disease have an average life expectancy, '
             'with wide variability, of about four years after diagnosis. There is no cure or '
             'medication to stop the disease from progressing, and people in the latter stages of '
             'DLB may be unable to care for themselves. Treatments aim to relieve some of the '
             'symptoms and reduce the burden on <a href="Caregiver" '
             'title="Caregiver">caregivers</a>. <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Acetylcholinesterase_inhibitors" title="Acetylcholinesterase '
             'inhibitors">Medicines</a> such as <a href="Donepezil" '
             'title="Donepezil">donepezil</a> and <a href="Rivastigmine" '
             'title="Rivastigmine">rivastigmine</a> can temporarily improve cognition and overall '
             'functioning, and <a href="Melatonin" title="Melatonin">melatonin</a> can be used for '
             'sleep-related symptoms. <a href="Antipsychotic" '
             'title="Antipsychotic">Antipsychotics</a> are usually avoided, even for '
             'hallucinations, because severe reactions occur in almost half of people with DLB, '
             'and their use can result in death. Management of the many different symptoms is '
             'challenging, as it involves multiple specialties and education of caregivers. </p> '
             '<div class="toclimit-4"><meta property="mw:PageProp/toc"/></div> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Classification_and_terminology">Classification and terminology</h2></div> '
             '<p>Dementia with Lewy bodies (DLB) is a type of <a href="Dementia" '
             'title="Dementia">dementia</a>, a group of diseases involving <a '
             'href="Progressive_disease" title="Progressive disease">progressive</a> <a '
             'href="Degenerative_disease" title="Degenerative disease">neurodegeneration</a> of '
             'the <a href="Central_nervous_system" title="Central nervous system">central nervous '
             'system</a>. It is one of the two <a href="Lewy_body_dementia" title="Lewy body '
             'dementia">Lewy body dementias</a>, along with <a '
             'href="Parkinson\'s_disease_dementia" title="Parkinson\'s disease '
             'dementia">Parkinson\'s disease dementia</a>. </p><p>Dementia with Lewy bodies can be '
             'classified in other ways. The <a class="mw-redirect" href="Parkinson_plus_syndrome" '
             'title="Parkinson plus syndrome">atypical parkinsonian syndromes</a> include DLB, '
             'along with other conditions. Also, DLB is a <a href="Synucleinopathy" '
             'title="Synucleinopathy">synucleinopathy</a>, meaning that it is characterized by '
             'abnormal deposits of <a href="Alpha-synuclein" '
             'title="Alpha-synuclein">alpha-synuclein</a> protein in the brain. The '
             'synucleinopathies include <a href="Parkinson\'s_disease" title="Parkinson\'s '
             'disease">Parkinson\'s disease</a>, <a href="Multiple_system_atrophy" title="Multiple '
             'system atrophy">multiple system atrophy</a>, and other rarer conditions. </p><p>The '
             'vocabulary of diseases associated with Lewy pathology causes confusion. <i>Lewy body '
             'dementia</i> (the umbrella term that encompasses the clinical diagnoses of dementia '
             "with Lewy bodies and Parkinson's disease dementia) differs from <i>Lewy body "
             'disease</i> (the term used to describe pathological findings of Lewy bodies on '
             "autopsy). Because individuals with Alzheimer's disease (AD) are often found on "
             'autopsy to also have <a href="Lewy_body" title="Lewy body">Lewy bodies</a>, DLB has '
             'been characterized as an Alzheimer disease-related dementia; the term <i>Lewy body '
             'variant of Alzheimer disease</i> is no longer used because the predominant pathology '
             "for these individuals is related to Alzheimer's disease. Even the term <i>Lewy body "
             'disease</i> may not describe the true nature of this group of diseases; a unique '
             'genetic architecture may predispose individuals to specific <i>diseases with Lewy '
             'bodies</i>, and naming controversies continue. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Signs_and_symptoms">Signs and symptoms</h2></div> <p>DLB is '
             'dementia that occurs with "some combination of fluctuating cognition, recurrent <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Visual_hallucinations" title="Visual '
             'hallucinations">visual hallucinations</a>, rapid eye movement (REM) sleep behavior '
             'disorder (RBD), and <a href="Parkinsonism" title="Parkinsonism">parkinsonism</a>", '
             "according to Armstrong (2019), when Parkinson's disease is <i>not</i> well "
             'established before the dementia occurs. DLB has widely varying symptoms and is more '
             'complex than many other dementias. Several areas of the <a href="Nervous_system" '
             'title="Nervous system">nervous system</a> (such as the <a '
             'href="Autonomic_nervous_system" title="Autonomic nervous system">autonomic nervous '
             'system</a> and numerous regions of the brain) can be affected by Lewy pathology, in '
             'which the alpha-synuclein deposits cause damage and corresponding neurologic '
             'deficits. </p><p>In DLB, there is an identifiable set of early signs and symptoms; '
             'these are called the <a href="Prodrome" title="Prodrome">prodromal</a>, or '
             'pre-dementia, phase of the disease. These early signs and symptoms can appear 15 '
             'years or more before dementia develops. The earliest symptoms are <a '
             'href="Constipation" title="Constipation">constipation</a> and <a href="Dizziness" '
             'title="Dizziness">dizziness</a> from <a href="Dysautonomia" '
             'title="Dysautonomia">autonomic dysfunction</a>, <a href="Hyposmia" '
             'title="Hyposmia">hyposmia</a> (reduced ability to smell), RBD, anxiety, and '
             'depression. RBD may appear years or decades before other symptoms. Memory loss is '
             'not always an early symptom. </p><p>Manifestations of DLB can be divided into '
             'essential, core, and supportive features. Dementia is the essential feature and must '
             'be present for diagnosis, while core and supportive features are further evidence in '
             'support of diagnosis (see <a href="#Criteria">diagnostic criteria below</a>). </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Essential_feature">Essential '
             'feature</h3></div> <p>A dementia diagnosis is made after cognitive decline '
             'progresses to a point of interfering with <a href="Activities_of_daily_living" '
             'title="Activities of daily living">normal daily activities</a>, or social or '
             'occupational function. While dementia is an essential feature of DLB, it does not '
             'always appear early on, and is more likely to be present as the condition '
             'progresses. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Core_features">Core '
             'features</h3></div> <p>While specific symptoms may vary, the core features of DLB '
             'are fluctuating cognition, alertness or attention; <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="REM_sleep_behavior_disorder" title="REM sleep behavior disorder">REM sleep '
             'behavior disorder</a>; one or more of the cardinal features of parkinsonism, not due '
             'to medication or stroke; and repeated visual hallucinations. </p><p>The 2017 '
             '<i>Fourth Consensus Report of the <a href="Dementia_with_Lewy_Bodies_Consortium" '
             'title="Dementia with Lewy Bodies Consortium">DLB Consortium</a></i> determined these '
             'to be core features based on the availability of high-quality evidence indicating '
             'they are highly specific to the condition. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading4"><h4 id="Fluctuating_cognition_and_alertness">Fluctuating cognition and '
             'alertness</h4></div> <p><i>Fluctuations</i> in cognitive function are the most '
             'characteristic feature of the Lewy body dementias. They are the most frequent '
             'symptom of DLB, and are often distinguishable from those of other dementias by '
             'concomitant fluctuations of attention and alertness, described by Tsamakis and '
             'Mueller (2021) as "spontaneous variations of cognitive abilities, alertness, or '
             'arousal". They are further distinguishable by a "marked amplitude between best and '
             'worst performances", according to McKeith (2002). These fluctuations vary in '
             'severity, frequency and duration; episodes last anywhere from seconds to weeks, '
             'interposed between periods of more normal functioning. When relatively lucid periods '
             'coincide with medical appointments, cognitive testing may inaccurately reflect '
             'disease severity, with subsequent assessments of cognition showing improvements from '
             'baseline. </p><p>Unlike the deficits in memory and orientation that are '
             "characteristic of Alzheimer's disease, the distinct impairments in cognition seen in "
             'DLB are most commonly in three domains: attention, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Executive_function" title="Executive function">executive function</a>, and <a '
             'href="Visuospatial_function" title="Visuospatial function">visuospatial '
             'function</a>. These fluctuating impairments are present early in the course of the '
             'disease. Individuals with DLB may be easily distracted, have a hard time focusing on '
             'tasks, or appear to be "delirium-like", "zoning out", or in states of altered '
             'consciousness with spells of confusion, agitation or incoherent speech. They may '
             'have disorganized speech and their ability to organize their thoughts may change '
             'during the day. </p><p>Executive function describes attentional and behavioral '
             'controls, memory and cognitive flexibility that aid problem solving and planning. '
             'Problems with executive function surface in activities requiring planning and '
             'organizing. Deficits can manifest in impaired job performance, inability to follow '
             'conversations, difficulties with multitasking, or mistakes in driving, such as '
             'misjudging distances or becoming lost. </p><p>The person with DLB may experience '
             'disorders of <a href="Wakefulness" title="Wakefulness">wakefulness</a> or <a '
             'href="Sleep_disorder" title="Sleep disorder">sleep disorders</a> (in addition to REM '
             'sleep behavior disorder) that can be severe. These disorders include daytime '
             'sleepiness, drowsiness or napping more than two hours a day, <a href="Insomnia" '
             'title="Insomnia">insomnia</a>, <a href="Periodic_limb_movement_disorder" '
             'title="Periodic limb movement disorder">periodic limb movements</a>, <a '
             'href="Restless_legs_syndrome" title="Restless legs syndrome">restless legs '
             'syndrome</a> and <a href="Sleep_apnea" title="Sleep apnea">sleep apnea</a>. </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="REM_sleep_behavior_disorder">REM sleep '
             'behavior disorder</h4></div> <div class="quotebox pullquote floatright" '
             'style="width:30%; ; color: #202122;background-color: beige;"> <div '
             'class="quotebox-title" style="color: #202122;background-color: beige;">REM sleep '
             'behavior disorder <br/>and dementia with Lewy bodies<div class="paragraphbreak" '
             'style="margin-top:0.5em"></div></div> <blockquote class="quotebox-quote '
             'left-aligned" style=""><p> "REM sleep behavior disorder (RBD) has been studied more '
             'thoroughly in correlation with DLB and is now considered a core feature.\xa0...\xa0'
             'Basically, dementia in the presence of polysomnogram-confirmed RBD suggests possible '
             'DLB."</p><div class="paragraphbreak" style="margin-top:0.5em"></div> </blockquote> '
             '<div style="padding-bottom: 0; padding-top: 0.5em"><cite class="left-aligned" '
             'style="">\xa0\xa0\xa0\xa0—B. Tousi (2017), <i>Diagnosis and Management of Cognitive '
             'and Behavioral Changes in Dementia With Lewy Bodies</i></cite></div> </div> <p>REM '
             'sleep behavior disorder (RBD) is a <a href="Parasomnia" '
             'title="Parasomnia">parasomnia</a> in which individuals lose the paralysis of muscles '
             '(atonia) that is normal during <a href="Rapid_eye_movement_sleep" title="Rapid eye '
             'movement sleep">rapid eye movement (REM) sleep</a>, and consequently act out their '
             'dreams or make other abnormal movements or vocalizations. About 80% of those with '
             'DLB have RBD. Abnormal sleep behaviors may begin before cognitive decline is '
             'observed, and may appear decades before any other symptoms, often as the first '
             'clinical indication of DLB and an early sign of a <a href="Synucleinopathy" '
             'title="Synucleinopathy">synucleinopathy</a>. </p><p>On autopsy, 94 to 98% of '
             'individuals with <a href="Polysomnography" '
             'title="Polysomnography">polysomnography</a>-confirmed RBD have a '
             "synucleinopathy—most commonly DLB or Parkinson's disease in about equal proportions. "
             'More than three out of four people with RBD are diagnosed with a neurodegenerative '
             'condition within ten years, but additional neurodegenerative diagnoses may emerge up '
             'to 50\xa0years after RBD diagnosis. RBD may subside over time. </p><p>Individuals '
             'with RBD may not be aware that they act out their dreams. RBD behaviors may include '
             'yelling, screaming, laughing, crying, unintelligible talking, nonviolent flailing, '
             'or more violent punching, kicking, choking, or scratching. The reported dream '
             'enactment behaviors are frequently violent, and involve a theme of being chased or '
             'attacked. People with RBD may fall out of bed or injure themselves or their bed '
             'partners, which may cause bruises, fractures, or <a href="Subdural_hematoma" '
             'title="Subdural hematoma">subdural hematomas</a>. Because people are more likely to '
             'remember or report violent dreams and behaviors—and to be referred to a specialist '
             'when injury occurs—<a href="Recall_bias" title="Recall bias">recall</a> or selection '
             'bias may explain the prevalence of violence reported in RBD. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Parkinsonism">Parkinsonism</h4></div> <p><a '
             'href="Parkinsonism" title="Parkinsonism">Parkinsonism</a> is a clinical <a '
             'href="Syndrome" title="Syndrome">syndrome</a> characterized by slowness of movement '
             '(called <i><a class="mw-redirect" href="Bradykinesia" '
             'title="Bradykinesia">bradykinesia</a></i>), <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Cogwheel_rigidity" title="Cogwheel rigidity">rigidity</a>, <a '
             'href="Balance_disorder" title="Balance disorder">postural instability</a>, and <a '
             'href="Tremor" title="Tremor">tremor</a>; it is found in DLB and many other '
             "conditions like Parkinson's disease, Parkinson's disease dementia, and others. "
             'Parkinsonism occurs in more than 85% of people with DLB, who may have one or more of '
             "these cardinal features, although tremor at rest is less common than in Parkinson's "
             'disease. </p><p>Motor symptoms may include <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Shuffling_gait" title="Shuffling gait">shuffling gait</a>, problems with '
             'balance, <a href="Falls_in_older_adults" title="Falls in older adults">falls</a>, '
             'blank expression, reduced range of facial expression, and low speech volume or a <a '
             'href="Hypophonia" title="Hypophonia">weak voice</a>. Presentation of motor symptoms '
             'is variable, but they are usually symmetric, presenting on both sides of the body. '
             'Only one of the cardinal symptoms of parkinsonism may be present, and the symptoms '
             "may be less severe than in persons with Parkinson's disease. </p> <div "
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Visual_hallucinations">Visual '
             'hallucinations</h4></div> <p>Up to 80% of people with DLB have visual '
             'hallucinations, typically early in the course of the disease. They are recurrent and '
             'frequent; may be scenic, elaborate and detailed; and usually involve animated '
             'perceptions of animals or people, including children and family members. Examples of '
             'visual hallucinations "vary from \'little people\' who casually walk around the '
             "house, 'ghosts' of dead parents who sit quietly at the bedside, to 'bicycles' that "
             'hang off of trees in the back yard". </p><p>These hallucinations can sometimes '
             'provoke fear, although their content is more typically neutral. In some cases, the '
             'person with DLB has insight that the hallucinations are not real. Among those with '
             'more disrupted cognition, the hallucinations can become more complex, and they may '
             'be less aware that their hallucinations are not real. Visual misperceptions or '
             'illusions are also common in DLB but differ from visual hallucinations. While visual '
             'hallucinations occur in the absence of real stimuli, visual illusions occur when '
             'real stimuli are incorrectly perceived; for example, a person with DLB may '
             'misinterpret a floor lamp for a person. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Supportive_features">Supportive features</h3></div> <p>Supportive features of '
             'DLB have less diagnostic weight, but they provide evidence for the diagnosis. '
             'Supportive features may be present early in the progression, and persist over time; '
             'they are common but they are not specific to the diagnosis. The supportive features '
             'are: </p> <ul><li>marked sensitivity to <a class="mw-redirect" href="Antipsychotics" '
             'title="Antipsychotics">antipsychotics</a> (neuroleptics);</li> <li>marked <a '
             'href="Dysautonomia" title="Dysautonomia">dysautonomia</a> (autonomic dysfunction) in '
             'which the <a href="Autonomic_nervous_system" title="Autonomic nervous '
             'system">autonomic nervous system</a> does not work properly;</li> <li>hallucinations '
             'in senses other than vision (hearing, <a href="Tactile_hallucination" title="Tactile '
             'hallucination">touch</a>, <a href="Hallucination#Gustatory" '
             'title="Hallucination">taste</a>, and <a href="Hallucination#Olfactory" '
             'title="Hallucination">smell</a>);</li> <li>hypersomnia (excessive sleepiness);</li> '
             '<li>hyposmia (reduced ability to smell);</li> <li><a href="Delusion" '
             'title="Delusion">delusions</a> (fixed false beliefs) organized around a common '
             'theme;</li> <li><a href="Balance_disorder" title="Balance disorder">postural '
             'instability</a>, loss of consciousness, and frequent falls;</li> <li>apathy, <a '
             'href="Anxiety" title="Anxiety">anxiety</a>, or <a href="Depression_(mood)" '
             'title="Depression (mood)">depression</a>.</li></ul> <figure class="mw-default-size '
             'mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="A sample of haloperidol under the '
             'trademark Haldol, 5 mg/ml" class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="2690" '
             'data-file-width="4357" decoding="async" height="154" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Haloperidol_(Haldol).jpg" width="250"/><figcaption>People with DLB are very '
             'sensitive to antipsychotic medications like <a href="Haloperidol" '
             'title="Haloperidol">haloperidol</a>, which carry an increased risk of <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Morbidity" title="Morbidity">morbidity</a> and <a '
             'href="Mortality_rate" title="Mortality rate">mortality</a> in '
             'DLB.</figcaption></figure> <p>Partly because of loss of cells that release the <a '
             'href="Neurotransmitter" title="Neurotransmitter">neurotransmitter</a> <a '
             'href="Dopamine" title="Dopamine">dopamine</a>, people with DLB may have <a '
             'href="Neuroleptic_malignant_syndrome" title="Neuroleptic malignant '
             'syndrome">neuroleptic malignant syndrome</a>, impairments in cognition or alertness, '
             'or irreversible exacerbation of parkinsonism including severe rigidity, and '
             'dysautonomia from the use of antipsychotics. </p><p>Dysautonomia (autonomic '
             'dysfunction) occurs when Lewy pathology affects the peripheral autonomic nervous '
             'system (the nerves dealing with the unconscious functions of organs such as the '
             'intestines, heart, and urinary tract). The first signs of autonomic dysfunction are '
             'often subtle. Manifestations include blood pressure problems such as <a '
             'href="Orthostatic_hypotension" title="Orthostatic hypotension">orthostatic '
             'hypotension</a> (significantly reduced blood pressure upon standing) and supine '
             'hypertension (significantly elevated blood pressure when lying horizontally); '
             'constipation, urinary problems, and <a href="Sexual_dysfunction" title="Sexual '
             'dysfunction">sexual dysfunction</a>; loss of or reduced ability to smell; and <a '
             'href="Hyperhidrosis" title="Hyperhidrosis">excessive sweating</a>, drooling, or <a '
             'href="Hypersalivation" title="Hypersalivation">salivation</a>, and problems '
             'swallowing (<a href="Dysphagia" title="Dysphagia">dysphagia</a>). '
             '</p><p>Alpha-synuclein deposits can affect <a href="Cardiac_muscle" title="Cardiac '
             'muscle">cardiac muscle</a> and blood vessels. "Degeneration of the cardiac '
             'sympathetic nerves is a neuropathological feature" of the Lewy body dementias, '
             'according to Yamada <span class="nowrap"><i>et al.</i></span> Almost all people with '
             'synucleinopathies have cardiovascular dysfunction, although most are asymptomatic. '
             'Between <span class="nowrap">50 and 60%</span> of individuals with DLB have '
             'orthostatic hypotension due to reduced blood flow, which can result in '
             'lightheadedness, feeling faint, and blurred vision. </p><p>From chewing to <a '
             'href="Defecation" title="Defecation">defecation</a>, alpha-synuclein deposits affect '
             'every level of <a href="Gastrointestinal_disease" title="Gastrointestinal '
             'disease">gastrointestinal function</a>. Almost all persons with DLB have upper '
             'gastrointestinal tract dysfunction (such as <a href="Gastroparesis" '
             'title="Gastroparesis">gastroparesis</a>, delayed gastric emptying) or lower '
             'gastrointestinal dysfunction (such as constipation and prolonged stool transit '
             'time). Persons with Lewy body dementia almost universally experience nausea, gastric '
             'retention, or abdominal distention from delayed gastric emptying. Problems with '
             'gastrointestinal function can affect medication absorption. Constipation can present '
             'a decade before diagnosis, and is one of the most common symptoms for people with '
             'Lewy body dementia. Dysphagia is milder than in other synucleinopathies and presents '
             'later. Urinary difficulties (<a href="Urinary_retention" title="Urinary '
             'retention">urinary retention</a>, <a href="Nocturia" title="Nocturia">waking at '
             'night to urinate</a>, increased urinary frequency and urgency, and over- or '
             'underactive bladder) typically appear later and may be mild or moderate. Sexual '
             'dysfunction usually appears early in synucleinopathies, and may include <a '
             'href="Erectile_dysfunction" title="Erectile dysfunction">erectile dysfunction</a> '
             'and difficulty achieving orgasm or ejaculating. </p><p>Among the other supportive '
             'features, psychiatric symptoms are often present when the individual first comes to '
             "clinical attention and are more likely, compared to Alzheimer's disease, to cause "
             'more impairment. About one-third of people with DLB have depression, and they often '
             'have anxiety as well. Anxiety leads to increased risk of falls, and apathy may lead '
             'to less social interaction. </p><p><a href="Agitation_(dementia)" title="Agitation '
             '(dementia)">Agitation</a>, behavioral disturbances, and delusions typically appear '
             'later in the course of the disease. Delusions may have a <a href="Paranoia" '
             'title="Paranoia">paranoid</a> quality, involving themes like a house being broken in '
             'to, infidelity, or abandonment. Individuals with DLB who misplace items may have '
             'delusions about theft. <a href="Capgras_delusion" title="Capgras delusion">Capgras '
             'delusion</a> may occur, in which the person with DLB loses knowledge of the spouse, '
             "caregiver, or partner's face, and is convinced that an imposter has replaced them. "
             'Hallucinations in other modalities are sometimes present, but are less frequent. '
             '</p><p>Sleep disorders (disrupted sleep cycles, sleep apnea, and arousal from '
             'periodic limb movement disorder) are common in DLB and may lead to hypersomnia. Loss '
             'of sense of smell may occur several years before other symptoms. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Causes">Causes</h2></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="See '
             'caption." class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="2391" data-file-width="4495" '
             'decoding="async" height="122" loading="lazy" src="./ApoE_22k_fragment_2001.png" '
             'width="230"/><figcaption>A ribbon diagram of <a href="Apolipoprotein_E" '
             'title="Apolipoprotein E">apolipoprotein E</a>. Variants of this protein influence '
             'the risk of developing DLB.</figcaption></figure> <p>The exact cause of DLB is '
             'unknown. Synucleinopathies are typically caused by interactions of <a '
             'href="Heredity" title="Heredity">genetic</a> and <a href="Exposome" '
             'title="Exposome">environmental influences</a>. Most people with DLB do not have '
             'affected family members, although occasionally DLB runs in a family. The <a '
             'href="Heritability" title="Heritability">heritability</a> of DLB is thought to be '
             'around 30% (that is, about 70% of disease severity is due to external factors or '
             'chance). </p><p>There is overlap in the genetic <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Risk_factor_(epidemiology)" title="Risk factor (epidemiology)">risk '
             'factors</a> for DLB, <a href="Alzheimer\'s_disease" title="Alzheimer\'s '
             'disease">Alzheimer\'s disease</a> (AD), Parkinson\'s disease, and Parkinson\'s '
             'disease dementia. The <i><a href="Apolipoprotein_E#Gene" title="Apolipoprotein '
             'E">APOE</a></i> gene has three common variants. One, <i>APOE</i> ε4, is a risk '
             'factor for DLB and AD, whereas <i>APOE</i> ε2 may be protective against both. '
             'Mutations in <i>GBA</i>, a gene for a lysosomal enzyme, are associated with both DLB '
             'and Parkinson\'s disease. Rarely, mutations in <i><a href="Alpha-synuclein" '
             'title="Alpha-synuclein">SNCA</a></i>, the gene for alpha-synuclein, or <i>LRRK2</i>, '
             "a gene for a kinase enzyme, can cause any of DLB, AD, Parkinson's disease or "
             "Parkinson's disease dementia. This suggests some shared genetic pathology may "
             'underlie all four diseases. </p><p>The greatest risk of developing DLB is being over '
             "the age of 50. Having REM sleep behavior disorder or Parkinson's disease confers a "
             'higher risk for developing DLB. The risk of developing DLB has not been linked to '
             'any specific lifestyle factors. Risk factors for rapid conversion of RBD to a '
             'synucleinopathy include impairments in color vision or the ability to smell, <a '
             'href="Mild_cognitive_impairment" title="Mild cognitive impairment">mild cognitive '
             'impairment</a>, and abnormal dopaminergic imaging. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Pathophysiology">Pathophysiology</h2></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="See cation." '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="750" data-file-width="750" '
             'decoding="async" height="230" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Immunostaining_(brown)_of_alpha-synuclein_in_Lewy_Bodies_and_Lewy_Neurites_in_the_neocortex_of_a_patient_with_Lewy_Body_Disease.jpg" '
             'width="230"/><figcaption>This <a href="Micrograph#Photomicrograph" '
             'title="Micrograph">photomicrograph</a> shows brown-<a href="Immunostaining" '
             'title="Immunostaining">immunostained</a> alpha-synuclein in <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Lewy_bodies" title="Lewy bodies">Lewy bodies</a> (large clumps) and Lewy '
             'neurites (thread-like structures) in the <a href="Neocortex" '
             'title="Neocortex">neocortical</a> tissue of a person who died with Lewy body '
             'disease.</figcaption></figure> <p>DLB is characterized by the development of '
             'abnormal collections of alpha-synuclein protein within diseased brain <a '
             'href="Neuron" title="Neuron">neurons</a>, manifesting as Lewy bodies and Lewy '
             'neurites. When these clumps of protein form, neurons function less optimally and '
             'eventually die. Neuronal loss in DLB leads to profound dopamine dysfunction and '
             'marked <a href="Cholinergic" title="Cholinergic">cholinergic</a> pathology; other '
             'neurotransmitters might be affected, but less is known about them. Damage in the '
             'brain is widespread, and affects many domains of functioning. </p><p>Loss of <a '
             'href="Acetylcholine" title="Acetylcholine">acetylcholine</a>-producing neurons is '
             'thought to account for degeneration in memory and learning, while the death of '
             'dopamine-producing neurons appears to be responsible for <a '
             'href="Behavior_change_(individual)" title="Behavior change '
             '(individual)">degeneration of behavior</a>, cognition, mood, movement, motivation, '
             'and sleep. The extent of Lewy body neuronal damage is a key determinant of dementia '
             'in the Lewy body disorders. </p><p>The precise mechanisms contributing to DLB are '
             'not well understood and are a matter of some controversy. The role of '
             'alpha-synuclein deposits is unclear, because individuals with no signs of DLB have '
             'been found on autopsy to have advanced alpha-synuclein pathology. The relationship '
             'between Lewy pathology and widespread cell death is contentious. It is not known if '
             'the pathology spreads between cells or follows another pattern. The mechanisms that '
             'contribute to cell death, how the disease advances through the brain, and the timing '
             'of cognitive decline are all poorly understood. There is no model to account for the '
             'specific neurons and brain regions that are affected. </p><p>Autopsy studies and '
             'amyloid imaging studies using <a href="Pittsburgh_compound_B" title="Pittsburgh '
             'compound B">Pittsburgh compound B</a> (PiB) indicate that tau protein pathology and '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="Amyloid_plaque" title="Amyloid plaque">amyloid '
             "plaques</a>, which are hallmarks of Alzheimer's disease, are also common in DLB and "
             'more common than in Parkinson\'s disease dementia. <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Amyloid-beta" title="Amyloid-beta">Amyloid-beta</a> (Aβ) deposits are found in '
             'the <a href="Tauopathy" title="Tauopathy">tauopathies</a>—neurodegenerative diseases '
             'characterized by <a class="mw-redirect" href="Neurofibrillary_tangles" '
             'title="Neurofibrillary tangles">neurofibrillary tangles</a> of hyperphosphorylated '
             'tau protein—but the mechanism underlying dementia is often mixed, and Aβ is also a '
             'factor in DLB. </p><p>A proposed <a href="Pathophysiology" '
             'title="Pathophysiology">pathophysiology</a> for REM sleep behavior disorder (RBD) '
             'implicates neurons in the <a '
             'href="Reticular_formation#Ascending_reticular_activating_system" title="Reticular '
             'formation">reticular formation</a> that regulate REM sleep. RBD might appear decades '
             'earlier than other symptoms in the Lewy body dementias because these cells are '
             'affected earlier, before spreading to other brain regions. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Diagnosis">Diagnosis</h2></div> <p>Dementia '
             'with Lewy bodies can only be definitively diagnosed after death with an autopsy of '
             'the brain (or in rare familial cases, via a genetic test), so diagnosis of the '
             'living is referred to as <i>probable</i> or <i>possible</i>. </p><p>Diagnosing DLB '
             'can be challenging because of the wide range of symptoms with differing levels of '
             'severity in each individual. DLB is often misdiagnosed or, in its early stages, '
             "confused with Alzheimer's disease (AD). The majority of individuals with Lewy body "
             'dementias receive an inaccurate initial diagnosis—such as AD, parkinsonism, other '
             'dementias or a psychiatric diagnosis—resulting in reduced support and increased fear '
             'and uncertainty, sometimes for many years. Comparing the rates of detection of DLB '
             'in autopsy studies to those diagnosed while in clinical care indicates that as many '
             'as one in three diagnoses of DLB may be missed. Another complicating factor is that '
             'DLB commonly occurs along with AD; autopsy reveals that half of people with DLB have '
             'some level of changes attributed to AD in their brains, which contributes to the '
             'wide-ranging variety of symptoms and diagnostic difficulty. </p><p>Living with an '
             'uncertain diagnosis and prognosis is a concern expressed by both individuals with '
             'DLB and their caregivers and difficulty gaining a diagnosis and differing '
             'interactions with healthcare professionals are common experiences; once diagnosed, '
             'there are still difficulties finding a doctor knowledgeable in treating DLB. Despite '
             'the difficulty in diagnosis, a prompt diagnosis is important because of the serious '
             'risks of sensitivity to antipsychotics and the need to inform both the person with '
             'DLB and the person\'s <a href="Caregiver" title="Caregiver">caregivers</a> about '
             "those medications' side effects. The management of DLB is difficult in comparison to "
             'many other neurodegenerative diseases, so an accurate diagnosis is important. </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Criteria">Criteria</h3></div> <p>The '
             '2017 <i>Fourth Consensus Report</i> established diagnostic criteria for probable and '
             'possible DLB, recognizing advances in detection since the earlier <i>Third '
             'Consensus</i> (2005) version. The 2017 criteria are based on essential, core, and '
             'supportive clinical features, and diagnostic <a href="Biomarker" '
             'title="Biomarker">biomarkers</a>. </p><p>The essential feature is dementia; for a '
             'DLB diagnosis, it must be significant enough to interfere with social or '
             'occupational functioning. </p> <figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" '
             'typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="A man is lying on his back, about to go inside a '
             'cylindrical chamber." class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="1688" '
             'data-file-width="2430" decoding="async" height="208" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./CSIRO_ScienceImage_9370_Amyloid_beta_plaque_in_the_brain_follows_specific_patterns_as_detected_by_CSIROs_analysis_technique_from_PiB_PET_scans_here_an_AIBL_volunteer_is_receiving_a_PiB_PET_scan.jpg" '
             'width="300"/><figcaption><a href="Positron_emission_tomography" title="Positron '
             'emission tomography">Positron emission tomography</a>, for example, using <a '
             'href="Pittsburgh_compound_B" title="Pittsburgh compound B">PiB</a> is helpful in the '
             'diagnosis of DLB.</figcaption></figure> <p>The four core clinical features '
             '(described in the <a href="#Signs_and_symptoms"><i>Signs and symptoms</i> '
             'section</a>) are fluctuating cognition, <a href="Hallucination#Visual" '
             'title="Hallucination">visual hallucinations</a>, <a '
             'href="Rapid_eye_movement_sleep_behavior_disorder" title="Rapid eye movement sleep '
             'behavior disorder">REM sleep behavior disorder</a>, and signs of <a '
             'href="Parkinsonism" title="Parkinsonism">parkinsonism</a>. Supportive clinical '
             'features are marked sensitivity to antipsychotics; marked <a href="Dysautonomia" '
             'title="Dysautonomia">autonomic dysfunction</a>; nonvisual hallucinations; '
             'hypersomnia (excessive sleepiness); <a href="Hyposmia" title="Hyposmia">hyposmia</a> '
             '(reduced ability to smell); false beliefs and delusions organized around a common '
             'theme; postural instability, loss of consciousness and frequent falls; and apathy, '
             'anxiety, or depression. </p><p>Direct laboratory-measurable biomarkers for DLB '
             'diagnosis are not known, but several indirect methods can lend further evidence for '
             'diagnosis. The indicative diagnostic biomarkers are: reduced <a '
             'href="Dopamine_transporter" title="Dopamine transporter">dopamine transporter</a> '
             'uptake in the <a href="Basal_ganglia" title="Basal ganglia">basal ganglia</a> shown '
             'on <a href="Positron_emission_tomography" title="Positron emission '
             'tomography">PET</a> or <a href="Single-photon_emission_computed_tomography" '
             'title="Single-photon emission computed tomography">SPECT</a> imaging; low uptake of '
             'iodine-<a href="Iobenguane" title="Iobenguane">metaiodobenzylguanidine</a> <span '
             'class="nowrap">(I-MIBG)</span> shown on <a class="mw-redirect" href="Myocardial" '
             'title="Myocardial">myocardial</a> <a href="Scintigraphy" '
             'title="Scintigraphy">scintigraphy</a>; and loss of atonia during REM sleep evidenced '
             'on polysomnography. Supportive diagnostic biomarkers (from PET, SPECT, <a '
             'href="CT_scan" title="CT scan">CT</a>, or <a href="Magnetic_resonance_imaging" '
             'title="Magnetic resonance imaging">MRI</a> brain imaging studies or <a '
             'href="Electroencephalography" title="Electroencephalography">EEG</a> monitoring) '
             'are: lack of damage to <a href="Temporal_lobe" title="Temporal lobe">medial temporal '
             "lobe</a> (damage is more likely in Alzheimer's disease); reduced <a "
             'href="Occipital_lobe" title="Occipital lobe">occipital</a> activity; and prominent '
             '<a href="Slow-wave_sleep" title="Slow-wave sleep">slow-wave</a> activity on EEG. '
             '</p><p>Probable DLB can be diagnosed when dementia and at least two core features '
             'are present, or when one core feature and at least one indicative biomarker are '
             'present. Possible DLB can be diagnosed when dementia and only one core feature are '
             'present or, if no core features are present, then at least one indicative biomarker '
             'is present. </p><p>DLB is distinguished from <a href="Parkinson\'s_disease_dementia" '
             'title="Parkinson\'s disease dementia">Parkinson\'s disease dementia</a> by the time '
             'frame in which dementia symptoms appear relative to <a href="Parkinsonism" '
             'title="Parkinsonism">parkinsonian</a> symptoms. DLB is diagnosed when cognitive '
             "symptoms begin before or at the same time as parkinsonian motor signs. Parkinson's "
             "disease dementia would be the diagnosis when Parkinson's disease is well established "
             'before the dementia occurs (the onset of dementia is more than a year after the '
             'onset of parkinsonian symptoms). Known as the <i>one-year rule</i>, the distinction '
             'is acknowledged to be arbitrary; it recognizes overlap between the conditions along '
             'with key differences, while allowing for variations in treatment and prognosis and '
             'providing a framework for research. </p><p>DLB is listed in the <i>Diagnostic and '
