             'Statistical Manual of Mental Disorders, Fifth Edition</i> (<a href="DSM-5" '
             'title="DSM-5">DSM-5</a>) as major or mild <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Cognitive_disorder" title="Cognitive disorder">neurocognitive disorder</a> '
             'with Lewy bodies. The differences between the DSM and DLB Consortium diagnostic '
             'criteria are: 1) the DSM does not include low dopamine transporter uptake as a '
             'supportive feature, and 2) unclear diagnostic weight is assigned to biomarkers in '
             'the DSM. Lewy body dementias are classified by the <a '
             'href="World_Health_Organization" title="World Health Organization">World Health '
             'Organization</a> in its <a href="ICD-11" title="ICD-11">ICD-11</a>, the <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="International_Statistical_Classification_of_Diseases_and_Related_Health_Problems" '
             'title="International Statistical Classification of Diseases and Related Health '
             'Problems">International Statistical Classification of Diseases and Related Health '
             'Problems</a>, in chapter 06, as neurodevelopmental disorders, code 6D82. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Clinical_history_and_testing">Clinical '
             'history and testing</h3></div> <p>Diagnostic tests can be used to establish some '
             'features of the condition and distinguish them from symptoms of other conditions. '
             'Diagnosis may include taking the person\'s <a href="Medical_history" title="Medical '
             'history">medical history</a>, a physical exam, assessment of neurological function, '
             'brain imaging, <a href="Neuropsychological_test" title="Neuropsychological '
             'test">neuropsychological testing</a> to assess cognitive function, <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Sleep_studies" title="Sleep studies">sleep studies</a>, '
             'myocardial scintigraphy, or laboratory testing to rule out conditions that may cause '
             'symptoms similar to dementia, such as abnormal <a href="Thyroid_function_tests" '
             'title="Thyroid function tests">thyroid function</a>, <a href="Syphilis" '
             'title="Syphilis">syphilis</a>, <a href="HIV" title="HIV">HIV</a>, and vitamin '
             'deficiencies. </p><p>Typical dementia screening tests used are the <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Mini-mental_state_examination" title="Mini-mental state '
             'examination">mini-mental state examination</a> (MMSE) and the Montreal Cognitive '
             'Assessment (MoCA). The pattern of cognitive impairment in DLB is distinct from other '
             'dementias, such as AD; the MMSE mainly tests for the memory and language impairments '
             'more commonly seen in those other dementias and may be less suited for assessing '
             'cognition in the Lewy body dementias, where testing of visuospatial and executive '
             'function is indicated. The MoCA may be better suited to assessing cognitive function '
             'in DLB, and the Clinician Assessment of Fluctuation scale and the Mayo Fluctuation '
             'Composite Score may help understand cognitive decline relative to fluctuations in '
             'DLB. For tests of attention, digit span, <a href="Serial_sevens" title="Serial '
             'sevens">serial sevens</a>, and spatial span can be used for simple screening, and '
             'the Revised Digit Symbol Subtest of the <a href="Wechsler_Adult_Intelligence_Scale" '
             'title="Wechsler Adult Intelligence Scale">Wechsler Adult Intelligence Scale</a> may '
             'show defects in attention that are characteristic of DLB. The <a '
             'href="Frontal_Assessment_Battery" title="Frontal Assessment Battery">Frontal '
             'Assessment Battery</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Stroop_test" title="Stroop '
             'test">Stroop test</a> and <a href="Wisconsin_Card_Sorting_Test" title="Wisconsin '
             'Card Sorting Test">Wisconsin Card Sorting Test</a> are used for evaluation of '
             'executive function, and there are many other screening instruments available. </p> '
             '<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="A '
             'plethora of wires are brought together in a box fastened just above the waist." '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="882" data-file-width="756" '
             'decoding="async" height="292" loading="lazy" src="./Polysomnography_connections.jpg" '
             'width="250"/><figcaption>Adult connected to wires from sensors for <a '
             'href="Polysomnography" '
             'title="Polysomnography">polysomnography</a></figcaption></figure> <p>If DLB is '
             'suspected when parkinsonism and dementia are the only presenting features, PET or '
             'SPECT imaging may show reduced dopamine transporter activity. A DLB diagnosis may be '
             'warranted if other conditions with reduced dopamine transporter uptake can be ruled '
             'out. </p><p>REM sleep behavior disorder(RBD) is diagnosed either by sleep study '
             'recording or, when sleep studies cannot be performed, by medical history and '
             'validated questionnaires. In individuals with dementia and a history of RBD, a '
             'probable DLB diagnosis can be justified (even with no other core feature or '
             'biomarker) based on a sleep study showing REM sleep without atonia because it is so '
             'highly predictive. Conditions similar to RBD, like severe sleep apnea and periodic '
             'limb movement disorder, must be ruled out. Prompt evaluation and treatment of RBD is '
             'indicated when a prior history of violence or injury is present as it may increase '
             'the likelihood of future violent dream enactment behaviors. Individuals with RBD may '
             'not be able to provide a history of dream enactment behavior, so bed partners are '
             'also consulted. The <a href="REM_Sleep_Behavior_Disorder_Single-Question_Screen" '
             'title="REM Sleep Behavior Disorder Single-Question Screen">REM Sleep Behavior '
             'Disorder Single-Question Screen</a> offers diagnostic sensitivity and specificity in '
             'the absence of polysomnography with one question: "Have you ever been told, or '
             "suspected yourself, that you seem to 'act out your dreams' while asleep (for "
             'example, punching, flailing your arms in the air, making running movements, etc.)?" '
             'Because some individuals with DLB do not have RBD, normal findings from a sleep '
             'study cannot rule out DLB. </p><p>Since 2001, iodine-<a href="Iobenguane" '
             'title="Iobenguane">metaiodobenzylguanidine</a> <span class="nowrap">(I-MIBG)</span> '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="Myocardial" title="Myocardial">myocardial</a> <a '
             'href="Scintigraphy" title="Scintigraphy">scintigraphy</a> has been used '
             'diagnostically in East Asia (principally Japan), but not in the US. MIBG is taken up '
             'by <a href="Sympathetic_nervous_system" title="Sympathetic nervous '
             'system">sympathetic</a> nerve endings, such as those that innervate the heart, and '
             'is <a href="Radioactive_tracer" title="Radioactive tracer">labeled</a> for '
             'scintigraphy with radioactive iodine. Autonomic dysfunction resulting from damage to '
             'nerves in the heart in patients with DLB is associated with lower cardiac uptake of '
             '<span class="nowrap">I-MIBG.</span> </p><p>There is no <a href="Genetic_testing" '
             'title="Genetic testing">genetic test</a> to determine if an individual will develop '
             'DLB and, according to the Lewy Body Dementia Association, genetic testing is not '
             'routinely recommended because there are only rare instances of hereditary DLB. </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Differential">Differential</h3></div> '
             '<p>Many neurodegenerative conditions share cognitive and motor symptoms with '
             'dementia with Lewy bodies. The <a href="Differential_diagnosis" title="Differential '
             'diagnosis">differential diagnosis</a> includes Alzheimer\'s disease; such <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Synucleinopathies" '
             'title="Synucleinopathies">synucleinopathies</a> as Parkinson\'s disease dementia, '
             "Parkinson's disease, and multiple system atrophy; vascular dementia; and <a "
             'href="Progressive_supranuclear_palsy" title="Progressive supranuclear '
             'palsy">progressive supranuclear palsy</a>, <a href="Corticobasal_degeneration" '
             'title="Corticobasal degeneration">corticobasal degeneration</a>, and <a '
             'href="Corticobasal_syndrome" title="Corticobasal syndrome">corticobasal '
             'syndrome</a>. </p><p>The symptoms of DLB are easily confused with delirium, or more '
             'rarely with psychosis; prodromal subtypes of delirium-onset DLB and '
             'psychiatric-onset DLB have been proposed. Mismanagement of delirium is a particular '
             'concern because of the risks to people with DLB associated with antipsychotics. A '
             'careful examination for features of DLB is warranted in individuals with unexplained '
             'delirium. PET or SPECT imaging showing reduced <a href="Dopamine_transporter" '
             'title="Dopamine transporter">dopamine transporter</a> uptake can help distinguish '
             'DLB from delirium. </p><p>Lewy pathology affects the peripheral autonomic nervous '
             'system; autonomic dysfunction is observed less often in AD, frontotemporal, or '
             'vascular dementias, so its presence can help differentiate them. MRI scans almost '
             'always show abnormalities in the brains of people with vascular dementia, which can '
             'begin suddenly. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 '
             'id="Alzheimer\'s_disease">Alzheimer\'s disease</h4></div> <p>Dementia with Lewy '
             "bodies (DLB) is distinguishable from Alzheimer's disease (AD) even in the prodromal "
             'phase. Short-term memory impairment is seen early in AD and is a prominent feature, '
             'while fluctuating attention is uncommon; impairment in DLB is more often seen first '
             'as fluctuating cognition. In contrast to AD—in which the <a href="Hippocampus" '
             'title="Hippocampus">hippocampus</a> is among the first brain structures affected, '
             'and <a href="Episodic_memory" title="Episodic memory">episodic memory</a> loss '
             'related to encoding of memories is typically the earliest symptom—memory impairment '
             'occurs later in DLB. People with <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Amnestic_mild_cognitive_impairment" title="Amnestic mild cognitive '
             'impairment">amnestic mild cognitive impairment</a> (in which memory loss is the main '
             'symptom) may progress to AD, whereas those with non-amnestic mild cognitive '
             'impairment (which has more prominent impairments in language, visuospatial, and '
             'executive domains) are more likely to progress towards DLB. Memory loss in DLB has a '
             'different progression from AD because <a href="Frontal_lobe" title="Frontal '
             'lobe">frontal</a> structures are involved earlier, with later involvement of <a '
             'href="Temporoparietal_junction" title="Temporoparietal junction">temporoparietal</a> '
             'brain structures. Verbal memory is not as severely affected as in AD. </p> <div '
             'class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" '
             'style="width:392px;max-width:392px"><div class="trow"><div class="theader"><span '
             'style="font-family:Sans-serif;font-size:100%;">Medical imaging in AD and '
             'DLB</span></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" '
             'style="width:390px;max-width:390px"><div class="thumbimage" '
             'style="border:none;;height:108px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="" '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="204" data-file-width="732" '
             'decoding="async" height="108" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./MRI_Alzheimer_Disease_and_Dementia_with_Lewy_Bodies.jpg" '
             'width="388"/></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">MRI of brain '
             'showing hippocampus atrophy (red rectangles), more prominent in AD than DLB, '
             'compared to normal control (NC)</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div '
             'class="tsingle" style="width:390px;max-width:390px"><div class="thumbimage" '
             'style="border:none;;height:152px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="" '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="289" data-file-width="735" '
             'decoding="async" height="153" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./FDG_Alzheimer_Disease_and_Dementia_with_Lewy_Bodies.jpg" '
             'width="388"/></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">FDG-PET '
             'horizontal cross section of brain, with brighter areas indicating higher metabolism. '
             'The cingulate island sign is indicated by the arrowhead.</div></div></div><div '
             'class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:390px;max-width:390px"><div '
             'class="thumbimage" style="border:none;;height:96px;overflow:hidden"><span '
             'typeof="mw:File"><img alt="" class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="182" '
             'data-file-width="735" decoding="async" height="96" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Surface_FDG_Alzheimer_Disease_and_Dementia_with_Lewy_Bodies.jpg" '
             'width="388"/></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">FDG-PET of '
             'brain surface, with the color red indicating areas of high metabolism. The occipital '
             'lobe in DLB (arrows) shows less activity than in AD.</div></div></div></div></div> '
             '<p>While 74% of people with autopsy-confirmed DLB had deficits in planning and '
             'organization, they show up in only 45% of people with AD. Visuospatial processing '
             'deficits are present in most individuals with DLB, and they show up earlier and are '
             'more pronounced than in AD. Hallucinations typically occur early in the course of '
             'DLB, are less common in early AD, but usually occur later in AD. AD pathology '
             'frequently co-occurs in DLB and is associated with more rapid decline; <a '
             'href="Cerebrospinal_fluid" title="Cerebrospinal fluid">cerebrospinal fluid</a> (CSF) '
             'testing may reveal an "Alzheimer\'s disease pattern" of higher tau and lower <a '
             'href="Amyloid_beta" title="Amyloid beta">amyloid beta</a>. </p><p>PET or SPECT '
             'imaging can be used to detect reduced dopamine transporter uptake and distinguish AD '
             'from DLB. Severe atrophy of the hippocampus is more typical of AD than DLB. Before '
             'dementia develops (during the mild cognitive impairment phase), MRI scans show '
             'normal hippocampal volume. After dementia develops, MRI shows more atrophy among '
             'individuals with AD, and a slower reduction in volume over time among people with '
             'DLB than those with AD. Compared to people with AD, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="FDG-PET" title="FDG-PET">FDG-PET</a> brain scans in people with DLB often show '
             'a <a href="Cingulate_island_sign" title="Cingulate island sign">cingulate island '
             'sign</a>. </p><p>In East Asia, particularly Japan,<span class="nowrap">I-MIBG</span> '
             'is used in the differential diagnosis of DLB and AD, because reduced labeling of '
             'cardiac nerves is seen only in Lewy body disorders. Other indicative and supportive '
             'biomarkers are useful in distinguishing DLB and AD (preservation of medial temporal '
             'lobe structures, reduced occipital activity, and slow-wave EEG activity). </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 '
             'id="Synucleinopathies">Synucleinopathies</h4></div> <p>Dementia with Lewy bodies and '
             "Parkinson's disease dementia are clinically similar after dementia occurs in "
             "Parkinson's disease. Delusions in Parkinson's disease dementia are less common than "
             "in DLB, and persons with Parkinson's disease are typically less caught up in their "
             'visual hallucinations than those with DLB. There is a lower incidence of tremor at '
             "rest in DLB than in Parkinson's disease, and signs of parkinsonism in DLB are more "
             'symmetrical. In multiple system atrophy, autonomic dysfunction appears earlier and '
             'is more severe, and is accompanied by uncoordinated movements, while visual '
             'hallucinations and fluctuating cognition are less common than in DLB. Urinary '
             'difficulty is one of the earliest symptoms with multiple system atrophy, and is '
             'often severe. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 '
             'id="Frontotemporal_dementias">Frontotemporal dementias</h4></div> <p>Corticobasal '
             'syndrome, corticobasal degeneration and progressive supranuclear palsy are <a '
             'href="Frontotemporal_dementia" title="Frontotemporal dementia">frontotemporal '
             'dementias</a> with features of parkinsonism and impaired cognition. Similar to DLB, '
             'imaging may show reduced dopamine transporter uptake. Corticobasal syndrome and '
             'degeneration, and progressive supranuclear palsy, are usually distinguished from DLB '
             'by history and examination. Motor movements in corticobasal syndrome are '
             'asymmetrical. There are differences in posture, gaze and facial expressions in the '
             'most common variants of progressive supranuclear palsy, and falling backwards is '
             'more common relative to DLB. Visual hallucinations and fluctuating cognition are '
             'unusual in corticobasal degeneration and progressive supranuclear palsy. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Management">Management</h2></div> <p><a '
             'href="Palliative_care" title="Palliative care">Palliative care</a> is offered to '
             'ameliorate symptoms, but there are no medications that can slow, stop, or improve '
             'the relentless progression of the disease. No medications for DLB are approved by '
             'the US <a href="Food_and_Drug_Administration" title="Food and Drug '
             'Administration">Food and Drug Administration</a> (FDA) as of 2023, although <a '
             'href="Donepezil" title="Donepezil">donepezil</a> is licensed in Japan and the '
             'Philippines for the treatment of DLB. As of 2020, there has been little study on the '
             'best management for non-motor symptoms such as sleep disorders and autonomic '
             'dysfunction; most information on management of autonomic dysfunction in DLB is based '
             "on studies of people with Parkinson's disease. </p><p>Management can be challenging "
             'because of the need to balance treatment of different symptoms: cognitive '
             'dysfunction, neuropsychiatric features, impairments related to the <a '
             'href="Extrapyramidal_system" title="Extrapyramidal system">motor system</a>, and '
             'other nonmotor symptoms. Individuals with DLB have widely different symptoms that '
             'fluctuate over time, and treating one symptom can worsen another; suboptimal care '
             'can result from a lack of coordination among the physicians treating different '
             'symptoms. A multidisciplinary approach—going beyond early and accurate diagnosis to '
             'include educating and supporting the caregivers—is favored. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Medication">Medication</h3></div> <div '
             'class="quotebox pullquote floatright" style="width:30%; ; color: '
             '#202122;background-color: beige;"> <div class="quotebox-title" style="color: '
             '#202122;background-color: beige;">Antipsychotic sensitivity <div '
             'class="paragraphbreak" style="margin-top:0.5em"></div></div> <blockquote '
             'class="quotebox-quote left-aligned" style=""><p> "The most fraught decision in the '
             'management of DLB relates to the use of antipsychotic medications ... DLB patients '
             'are particularly at risk of antipsychotic medication morbidity and '
             'mortality."</p><div class="paragraphbreak" style="margin-top:0.5em"></div> '
             '</blockquote> <div style="padding-bottom: 0; padding-top: 0.5em"><cite '
             'class="left-aligned" style="">\xa0\xa0\xa0\xa0—B.P. Boot (2015), <i>Comprehensive '
             'treatment of dementia with Lewy bodies</i></cite></div> </div> <p>Pharmacological '
             'management of DLB is complex because of adverse effects of medications and the wide '
             'range of symptoms to be treated (cognitive, motor, neuropsychiatric, autonomic, and '
             'sleep). <a href="Anticholinergic" title="Anticholinergic">Anticholinergic</a> and <a '
             'href="Dopaminergic" title="Dopaminergic">dopaminergic</a> agents can have adverse '
             'effects or result in psychosis in individuals with DLB, and a medication that '
             'addresses one feature might worsen another. For example, <a '
             'href="Acetylcholinesterase_inhibitor" title="Acetylcholinesterase '
             'inhibitor">acetylcholinesterase inhibitors</a> (AChEIs) for cognitive symptoms can '
             'lead to complications in dysautonomia features; treatment of movement symptoms with '
             '<a href="Dopamine_agonist" title="Dopamine agonist">dopamine agonists</a> may worsen '
             'neuropsychiatric symptoms; and treatment of hallucinations and psychosis with '
             'antipsychotics may worsen other symptoms or lead to a potentially fatal reaction. '
             '</p><p>Extreme caution is required in the use of antipsychotic medication in people '
             'with DLB because of their sensitivity to these agents. Severe and life-threatening '
             'reactions occur in almost half of people with DLB, and can be fatal after a single '
             'dose. Antipsychotics with <a href="Dopamine_receptor_D2" title="Dopamine receptor '
             'D2">D dopamine receptor</a>-blocking properties are used only with great caution. '
             'According to Boot (2013), "electing not to use neuroleptics is often the best course '
             'of action". People with Lewy body dementias who take neuroleptics are at risk for '
             'neuroleptic malignant syndrome, a life-threatening illness. There is no evidence to '
             'support the use of antipsychotics to treat the Lewy body dementias, and they carry '
             'the additional risk of stroke when used in the elderly with dementia. '
             '</p><p>Medications (including <a href="Tricyclic_antidepressant" title="Tricyclic '
             'antidepressant">tricyclic antidepressants</a> and treatments for <a '
             'href="Urinary_incontinence" title="Urinary incontinence">urinary incontinence</a>) '
             'with anticholinergic properties that cross the <a href="Blood%E2%80%93brain_barrier" '
             'title="Blood–brain barrier">blood–brain barrier</a> can cause memory loss. The <a '
             'href="Antihistamine" title="Antihistamine">antihistamine</a> medication <a '
             'href="Diphenhydramine" title="Diphenhydramine">diphenhydramine</a> (<a '
             'href="Benadryl" title="Benadryl">Benadryl</a>), sleep medications like <a '
             'href="Zolpidem" title="Zolpidem">zolpidem</a>, and <a href="Benzodiazepine" '
             'title="Benzodiazepine">benzodiazepines</a> may worsen confusion or <a '
             'href="Neuropsychiatry" title="Neuropsychiatry">neuropsychiatric</a> symptoms. Some '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="General_anesthetic" title="General anesthetic">general '
             'anesthetics</a> may cause confusion or delirium upon waking in persons with Lewy '
             'body dementias, and may result in permanent decline. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading4"><h4 id="Cognitive_symptoms">Cognitive symptoms</h4></div> <p>There is '
             'strong evidence for the use of acetylcholinesterase inhibitors (AChEIs) to treat '
             'cognitive problems; these medications include <a href="Rivastigmine" '
             'title="Rivastigmine">rivastigmine</a> and <a href="Donepezil" '
             'title="Donepezil">donepezil</a>. Both are first-line treatments in the UK. Even when '
             'the AChEIs do not lead to improvement in cognitive symptoms, people taking them may '
             'have less deterioration overall, although there may be adverse gastrointestinal '
             'effects. The use of these medications can reduce the burden on caregivers and '
             'improve <a href="Activities_of_daily_living" title="Activities of daily '
             'living">activities of daily living</a> for the individual with DLB. The AChEIs are '
             'initiated carefully as they may aggravate autonomic dysfunction or sleep behaviors. '
             'There is less evidence for the efficacy of <a href="Memantine" '
             'title="Memantine">memantine</a> in DLB, but it may be used alone or with an AChEI '
             'because of its low side effect profile. Anticholinergic drugs are avoided because '
             'they worsen cognitive symptoms. </p><p>To improve daytime alertness, there is mixed '
             'evidence for the use of stimulants such as <a href="Methylphenidate" '
             'title="Methylphenidate">methylphenidate</a> and <a href="Dextroamphetamine" '
             'title="Dextroamphetamine">dextroamphetamine</a>; although worsening of '
             'neuropsychiatric symptoms is not common, they can increase the risk of psychosis. <a '
             'href="Modafinil" title="Modafinil">Modafinil</a> and <a href="Armodafinil" '
             'title="Armodafinil">armodafinil</a> may be effective for daytime sleepiness. </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Motor_symptoms">Motor '
             'symptoms</h4></div> <p>Motor symptoms in DLB appear to respond somewhat less to '
             'medications used to treat Parkinson\'s disease, like <a href="Levodopa" '
             'title="Levodopa">levodopa</a>, and these medications can increase neuropsychiatric '
             'symptoms. Almost one out of every three individuals with DLB develops psychotic '
             'symptoms from levodopa. If such medications are needed for motor symptoms, cautious '
             'introduction with slow increases to the lowest possible dose may help avoid '
             'psychosis. </p><p>The anticonvulsant <a href="Zonisamide" '
             'title="Zonisamide">zonisamide</a> has been approved in Japan since 2009 for treating '
             "Parkinson's disease and since 2018 to treat parkinsonism in DLB. There is high "
             'certainty according to the GRADE certainty rating approach that it is effective for '
             'treating motor symptoms in DLB. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 '
             'id="Neuropsychiatric_symptoms">Neuropsychiatric symptoms</h4></div> '
             '<p>Neuropsychiatric symptoms of DLB (aggression, anxiety, apathy, delusions, '
             'depression and hallucinations) do not always require treatment. The first line of '
             'defense in decreasing visual hallucinations is to reduce the use of dopaminergic '
             'drugs, which can worsen hallucinations. If new neuropsychiatric symptoms appear, the '
             'use of medications (such as anticholinergics, tricyclic antidepressants, '
             'benzodiazepines and <a href="Opioid" title="Opioid">opioids</a>) that might be '
             'contributing to these symptoms is reviewed. </p><p>Among the AChEIs, donepezil and '
             'rivastigmine can help reduce neuropsychiatric symptoms and improve the frequency and '
             'severity of hallucinations in the less severe stages of DLB. For treating psychosis '
             'and agitation in DLB, there is low evidence for memantine, <a href="Olanzapine" '
             'title="Olanzapine">olanzapine</a> and <a href="Aripiprazole" '
             'title="Aripiprazole">aripiprazole</a>, and very low evidence for the efficacy of <a '
             'href="Quetiapine" title="Quetiapine">quetiapine</a>. Although <a href="Clozapine" '
             'title="Clozapine">clozapine</a> has been shown effective in Parkinson\'s disease, '
             'there is very low evidence for its use to treat visual hallucinations in DLB, and '
             'its use requires regular blood monitoring. </p><p>Apathy may be treated with AChEIs, '
             'and they may also reduce hallucinations, delusions, anxiety and agitation. Most '
             'medications to treat anxiety and depression have not been adequately investigated '
             'for DLB. Antidepressants may affect sleep and worsen RBD. <a href="Mirtazapine" '
             'title="Mirtazapine">Mirtazapine</a> and <a '
             'href="Selective_serotonin_reuptake_inhibitor" title="Selective serotonin reuptake '
             'inhibitor">SSRIs</a> can be used to treat depression, depending on how well they are '
             'tolerated, and guided by general advice for the use of antidepressants in dementia. '
             'Antidepressants with anticholinergic properties may worsen hallucinations and '
             'delusions. People with <a class="mw-redirect" href="Capgras_syndrome" title="Capgras '
             'syndrome">Capgras syndrome</a> may not tolerate AChEIs. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading4"><h4 id="Sleep_disorders">Sleep disorders</h4></div> <p>The first steps '
             'in managing sleep disorders are to evaluate the use of medications that impact sleep '
             'and provide education about <a href="Sleep_hygiene" title="Sleep hygiene">sleep '
             'hygiene</a>. Sleep medications are carefully evaluated for each individual as they '
             'carry increased risk of falls, increased daytime sleepiness, and worsening '
             'cognition. </p><p>Injurious dream enactment behaviors are a treatment priority. '
             'Frequency and severity of RBD may be lessened by treating sleep apnea, if it is '
             'present. RBD may be treated with <a href="Melatonin" title="Melatonin">melatonin</a> '
             'or <a href="Clonazepam" title="Clonazepam">clonazepam</a>. Melatonin may be more '
             'helpful in preventing injuries, and it offers a safer alternative, because '
             'clonazepam can produce deteriorating cognition, and worsen sleep apnea. '
             '</p><p>Memantine is useful for some people. Modafinil may be used for hypersomnia, '
             'but no trials support its use in DLB. Antidepressants (SSRIs, <a '
             'href="Serotonin%E2%80%93norepinephrine_reuptake_inhibitor" '
             'title="Serotonin–norepinephrine reuptake inhibitor">SNRIs</a>, tricyclics, and <a '
             'href="Monoamine_oxidase_inhibitor" title="Monoamine oxidase inhibitor">MAOIs</a>), '
             'AChEIs, <a href="Beta_blocker" title="Beta blocker">beta blockers</a>, <a '
             'href="Caffeine" title="Caffeine">caffeine</a>, and <a href="Tramadol" '
             'title="Tramadol">tramadol</a> may worsen RBD. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading4"><h4 id="Autonomic_symptoms">Autonomic symptoms</h4></div> <p>Decreasing '
             'the dosage of dopaminergic or <a href="Atypical_antipsychotic" title="Atypical '
             'antipsychotic">atypical antipsychotic</a> drugs may be needed with orthostatic '
             'hypotension, and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Antihypertensive_drug" '
             'title="Antihypertensive drug">high blood pressure drugs</a> can sometimes be '
             'stopped. When non-pharmacological treatments for orthostatic hypotension have been '
             'exhausted, <a href="Fludrocortisone" title="Fludrocortisone">fludrocortisone</a>, <a '
             'href="Droxidopa" title="Droxidopa">droxidopa</a>, or <a href="Midodrine" '
             'title="Midodrine">midodrine</a> are options, but these drugs have not been '
             'specifically studied for DLB as of 2020. Delayed gastric emptying can be worsened by '
             'dopaminergic medications, and constipation can be worsened by opiates and '
             'anticholinergic medications. <a href="Muscarinic_antagonist" title="Muscarinic '
             'antagonist">Muscarinic antagonists</a> used for urinary symptoms might worsen '
             'cognitive impairment in people with Lewy body dementias. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Other">Other</h3></div> <p>There is no high-quality evidence '
             'for non-pharmacological management of DLB, but some interventions have been shown '
             'effective for addressing similar symptoms that occur in other dementias. For '
             'example, organized activities, <a href="Music_therapy" title="Music therapy">music '
             'therapy</a>, physical activity and <a href="Occupational_therapy" '
             'title="Occupational therapy">occupational therapy</a> may help with psychosis or '
             'agitation, while exercise and gait training can help with motor symptoms. <a '
             'href="Cognitive_behavioral_therapy" title="Cognitive behavioral therapy">Cognitive '
             'behavioral therapy</a> can be tried for depression or hallucinations, although there '
             'is no evidence for its use in DLB. Cues can be used to help with memory retrieval. '
             '</p><p>For autonomic dysfunction, several non-medication strategies may be helpful. '
             'Dietary changes include avoiding meals high in fat and sugary foods, eating smaller '
             'and more frequent meals, after-meal walks, and increasing fluids or <a '
             'href="Dietary_fiber" title="Dietary fiber">dietary fiber</a> to treat constipation. '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="Stool_softener" title="Stool softener">Stool '
             'softeners</a> and exercise also help with constipation. Excess sweating can be '
             'helped by avoiding <a href="Alcohol_(drug)" title="Alcohol (drug)">alcohol</a> and '
             'spicy foods, and using cotton bedding and loose fitting clothing. </p><p>Physical '
             'exercise in a sitting or recumbent position, and exercise in a pool, can help '
             'maintain conditioning. <a class="mw-redirect" href="Compression_stocking" '
             'title="Compression stocking">Compression stockings</a> and elevating the head of the '
             'bed may also help, and increasing fluid intake or table salt can be tried to reduce '
             'orthostatic hypotension. To lessen the risk of fractures in individuals at risk for '
             'falls, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Bone_mineral_density" title="Bone mineral '
             'density">bone mineral density</a> screening and testing of <a '
             'href="Vitamin_D_deficiency" title="Vitamin D deficiency">vitamin D levels</a> are '
             'used, and caregivers are educated on the importance of <a href="Fall_prevention" '
             'title="Fall prevention">preventing falls</a>. <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Physiotherapy" title="Physiotherapy">Physiotherapy</a> has been shown helpful '
             "for Parkinson's disease dementia, but as of 2020, there is no evidence to support "
             'physical therapy in people with DLB. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Caregiving">Caregiving</h3></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" '
             'role="note">Further information: <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Caring_for_people_with_dementia" title="Caring for people with '
             'dementia">Caring for people with dementia</a></div> <p>Demands placed on <a '
             'href="Caregiver" title="Caregiver">caregivers</a> are higher than in Alzheimer\'s '
             'disease (AD) because of the neuropsychiatric symptoms associated with DLB. '
             'Contributing factors to the caregiver burden in DLB are <a href="Emotional_lability" '
             'title="Emotional lability">emotional fluctuations</a>, apathy, psychosis, '
             'aggression, agitation, and night-time behaviors such as parasomnias, that lead to a '
             'loss of independence earlier than in AD. Caregivers may experience depression and '
             'exhaustion, and they may need support from other people. Other family members who '
             'are not present in the <a href="Home_care" title="Home care">daily caregiving</a> '
             'may not observe the fluctuating behaviors or recognize the stress on the caregiver, '
             'and conflict can result when family members are not supportive. </p><p>Teaching '
             'caregivers how to manage neuropsychiatric symptoms (such as agitation and psychosis) '
             'is recommended, although education for caregivers has not been studied as thoroughly '
             "as in AD or Parkinson's disease. Caregiver education reduces not only distress for "
             'the caregiver, but symptoms for the individual with dementia. Caregiver training, <a '
             'href="Watchful_waiting" title="Watchful waiting">watchful waiting</a>, identifying '
             'sources of pain, and increasing social interaction can help minimize agitation. '
             'Individuals with dementia may not be able to communicate that they are in pain, and '
             'pain is a common trigger of agitation. </p><p>Visual hallucinations associated with '
             'DLB create a particular burden on caregivers. Caregivers can be educated to distract '
             'or change the subject when confronted with hallucinations, and that this is more '
             'effective than arguing over the reality of the hallucination. Coping strategies may '
             'help and are worth trying, even though there is no evidence for their efficacy. '
             'These strategies include having the person with DLB look away or look at something '
             'else, focus on or try to touch the hallucination, wait for it to go away on its own, '
             'and speak with others about the visualization. Delusions and hallucinations may be '
             'reduced by increasing lighting in the evening, and making sure there is no light at '
             'night when the individual with DLB is sleeping. </p><p>With the increased risk of '
             'side effects from antipsychotics for people with DLB, educated caregivers are able '
             'to act as advocates for the person with DLB. If evaluation or treatment in an '
             'emergency room is needed, the caregiver may be able to explain the risks associated '
             'with neuroleptic use for persons with DLB. Medical alert bracelets or notices about '
             'medication sensitivity are available and can save lives. </p><p>Individuals and '
             'their caregivers can be counselled about the need to improve bedroom safety for RBD '
             'symptoms. Sleep-related injuries from falling or jumping out of bed can be avoided '
             'by lowering the height of the bed, placing a mattress next to the bed to soften the '
             'impact of a fall, and removing sharp objects from around the bed. Sharp surfaces '
             'near the bed can be padded, bed alarm systems may help with sleepwalking, and bed '
             'partners may find it safer to sleep in another room. According to St Louis and '
             'Boeve, firearms should be locked away, out of the bedroom. </p><p>A home safety '
             'assessment can be done when there is risk of falling. Handrails and shower chairs '
             'can help avoid falls. Driving ability may be impaired early in DLB because of visual '
             'hallucinations, movement issues related to parkinsonism, and fluctuations in '
             'cognitive ability, and at some point it becomes unsafe for the person to drive. '
             'Driving ability is assessed as part of management, and family members generally '
             'determine when driving privileges are removed. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Prognosis">Prognosis</h2></div> <p>As of 2025, there is no '
             'disease-modifying treatment for dementia with Lewy bodies (DLB). The <a '
             'href="Prognosis" title="Prognosis">prognosis</a> for DLB has not been well studied; '
             'early studies had methodological limitations, such as small sample size and '
             "selection bias. Relative to Alzheimer's disease (AD) and other dementias, DLB "
             'generally leads to higher rates of disability, hospitalization and '
             'institutionalization, and lower life expectancy and <a href="Quality_of_life" '
             'title="Quality of life">quality of life</a>, with increased costs of care. '
             'Depression, apathy, and visual hallucinations contribute to the reduced quality of '
             'life. Decline may be more rapid when the <a href="Apolipoprotein" '
             'title="Apolipoprotein"><i>APOE</i> gene</a> is present, or when AD—or its '
             'biomarkers—is also present. The severity of orthostatic hypotension also predicts a '
             'worse prognosis. Visuospatial deficits early in the course of DLB were thought to be '
             'a predictor of rapid decline, but more recent studies did not find an association. '
             '</p><p>The trajectory of cognitive decline in DLB is difficult to establish because '
             'of the high rate of missed diagnoses; the typical delay of a year in the US, and 1.2 '
             'years in the UK, for diagnosis of DLB means that a baseline from which deterioration '
             'can be measured is often absent. Compared to AD, which is better studied, memory is '
             'thought to be retained longer, while verbal fluency may be lost faster, but the most '
             'common tools used to assess cognition may miss the most common cognitive deficits in '
             'DLB, and better studies are needed. There are more neuropsychiatric symptoms in DLB '
             'than AD, and they may emerge earlier, so those with DLB may have a less favorable '
             'prognosis, with more rapid cognitive decline, more admissions to residential care, '
             'and a lower life expectancy. An increased rate of hospitalization compared to AD is '
             'most commonly related to hallucinations and confusion, followed by falls and '
             'infection. </p><p>Life expectancy is difficult to predict, and limited study data '
             'are available. Survival may be defined from the point of disease onset, or from the '
             'point of diagnosis. There is wide variability in survival times, as DLB may rapidly '
             'or slowly progress. A 2019 meta-analysis found an average survival time after '
             'diagnosis of 4.1 years—indicating survival in DLB 1.6 years less than after a '
             "diagnosis of Alzheimer's disease. A 2017 review found survival from disease onset "
             'between 5.5 and 7.7 years, and survival from diagnosis between 1.9 and 6.3 years. '
             'The difference in survival between AD and DLB could be because DLB is more '
             'challenging to diagnose, and may be diagnosed later in the course of the disease. An '
             'online survey with 658 respondents found that, after diagnosis, more than 10% died '
             'within a year, 10% lived more than 7 years, and some lived more than 10 years. Some '
             'individuals with DLB live for 20 years. Shorter life expectancy is more likely when '
             'visual hallucinations, abnormal gait, and variable cognition are present early on. '
             '</p><p>Fear and anxiety feature strongly for both people with Lewy body dementia and '
             'their caregivers; a range of emotional responses to living with Lewy bodies includes '
             'fear of hallucinations, fear of falls, and frightening nightmares as a result of REM '
             'sleep behavior disorder, and being fearful of the effects of tiredness and fatigue. '
             'The symptoms of fluctuations, depression, delirium, and violence are also '
             'experienced as frightening. A lot of physical support from friends and family is '
             'often required to maintain social and supporting relationships. Individuals with '
             'Lewy body dementias describe feeling a burden in the wider social context, as they '
             'reduce attending social events due to their increasing physical needs. Frequently '
             'reported burden dimensions include personal strain and interference with personal '
             'life, which can lead to relationship dissatisfaction and resentment. </p><p>In the '
             'late phase of the disease, people may be unable to care for themselves. Falls—caused '
             'by many factors including parkinsonism, dysautonomia, and frailness—increase <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Morbidity" title="Morbidity">morbidity</a> and <a '
             'href="Mortality_rate" title="Mortality rate">mortality</a>. Failure to thrive and <a '
             'href="Aspiration_pneumonia" title="Aspiration pneumonia">aspiration pneumonia</a>, a '
             'complication of dysphagia (difficulty swallowing) that results from dysautonomia, '
             'commonly cause death among people with the Lewy body dementias. <a '
             'href="Cardiovascular_disease" title="Cardiovascular disease">Cardiovascular '
             'disease</a> and <a href="Sepsis" title="Sepsis">sepsis</a> are also common causes of '
             'death. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Epidemiology">Epidemiology</h2></div> <p>Dementia with Lewy bodies is the second '
             "most common type of dementia, after Alzheimer's disease (AD). </p><p>The diagnostic "
             'criteria for DLB before 2017 were <a href="Sensitivity_and_specificity" '
             'title="Sensitivity and specificity">highly specific, but not very sensitive</a>, so '
             'that more than half of cases were missed historically. Dementia with Lewy bodies was '
             'under-recognized as of 2021, and there is little data on its <a href="Epidemiology" '
             'title="Epidemiology">epidemiology</a>. The <a href="Incidence_(epidemiology)" '
             'title="Incidence (epidemiology)">incidence</a> and <a href="Prevalence" '
             'title="Prevalence">prevalence</a> of DLB are not known accurately, but estimates are '
             'increasing with better recognition of the condition since 2017. </p><p>About 0.4% of '
             'those over the age of 65 are affected with DLB, and between <span class="nowrap">1 '
             'and 4</span> per 1,000 people develop the condition each year. Symptoms usually '
             'appear between the ages of 50 and 80 (median 76), and it is not uncommon for it to '
             'be diagnosed before the age of 65. </p><p>DLB is thought to be slightly more common '
             'in men than women, but this finding has been challenged and is inconsistent across '
             'studies. Women may be over-represented in community samples and under-represented in '
             'clinical populations, where REM sleep behavior disorder (RBD) is more frequently '
             'diagnosed in men; the diagnosis appears to have a higher prevalence for men in those '
             'under 75, while women appear to be diagnosed later and with greater cognitive '
             'impairment. Studies in Japan, France and Britain show a more equal prevalence '
             'between men and women than in the US. </p><p>An estimated 10 to 15% of diagnosed '
             'dementias are Lewy body type, but estimates range as high as 23% for those in '
             'clinical studies. A French study found an incidence among persons 65\xa0years and '
             'older almost four times higher than a US study <span class="nowrap">(32 US vs 112 '
             'France</span> per 100,000 person-years), but the US study may have excluded people '
             'with only mild or no parkinsonism, while the French study screened for parkinsonism. '
             'Neither of the studies assessed systematically for RBD, so DLB may have been '
             'underdiagnosed in both studies. A door-to-door study in Japan found a prevalence of '
             '0.53% for persons 65 and older, and a Spanish study found similar results. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="History">History</h2></div> <p>Frederic Lewy '
             '(1885–1950) was the first to discover the abnormal protein deposits in the early '
             "1900s. In 1912, studying Parkinson's disease (<i>paralysis agitans</i>), he "
             'described findings of these <a href="Inclusion_bodies" title="Inclusion '
             'bodies">inclusion bodies</a> in the <a href="Vagus_nerve" title="Vagus nerve">vagus '
             'nerve</a>, the <a class="mw-redirect" href="Nucleus_basalis_of_Meynert" '
             'title="Nucleus basalis of Meynert">nucleus basalis of Meynert</a> and other brain '
             'regions. He published a book, <i>The Study on Muscle Tone and Movement. Including '
             'Systematic Investigations on the Clinic, Physiology, Pathology, and Pathogenesis of '
             'Paralysis agitans</i>, in 1923 and except for one brief paper a year later, never '
             'mentioned his findings again. </p><p>In 1961, Okazaki <i>et al.</i> published an '
             'account of diffuse Lewy-type inclusions associated with dementia in two autopsied '
             'cases. Dementia with Lewy bodies was fully described in an autopsied case by '
             'Japanese psychiatrist and neuropathologist Kenji Kosaka in 1976. Kosaka first '
             'proposed the term <i>Lewy body disease</i> four years later, based on 20\xa0'
             'autopsied cases. DLB was thought to be rare until it became easier to diagnose in '
             'the 1980s after the discovery of alpha-synuclein <a href="Immunostaining" '
             'title="Immunostaining">immunostaining</a> that highlighted Lewy bodies in <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Post_mortem" title="Post mortem">post mortem</a> brains. '
             '<span class="nowrap">Kosaka <i>et al.</i></span> described thirty-four more cases in '
             '1984, which were mentioned along with four UK cases by <span class="nowrap">Gibb '
             '<i>et al.</i></span> in 1987 in the journal <i>Brain</i>, bringing attention to the '
             'Japanese work in the Western world. A year later, <span class="nowrap">Burkhardt '
             '<i>et al.</i></span> published the first general description of diffuse Lewy body '
             'disease. </p><p>In the 1990s, with Japanese, UK, and US researchers finding that DLB '
             'was a common dementia, there were still no available diagnostic guidelines, with '
             'each group using different terminology. The different groups of researchers began to '
             'realize that a collaborative approach was needed if research was to advance. The <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="DLB_Consortium" title="DLB Consortium">DLB Consortium</a> '
             'was established, and, in 1996, the term <i>dementia with Lewy bodies</i> was agreed '
             'upon, and the first criteria for diagnosing DLB were elaborated. </p><p>Two 1997 '
             'discoveries highlighted the importance of Lewy body inclusions in neurodegenerative '
             'processes: a mutation in the <i>SNCA</i> gene that encodes the alpha-synuclein '
             "protein was found in kindreds with Parkinson's disease, and Lewy bodies and neurites "
             'were found to be <a href="Immunoassay" title="Immunoassay">immunoreactive</a> for '
             'alpha-synuclein. Thus, alpha-synuclein aggregation was established as the primary '
             'building block of the synucleinopathies. </p><p>Between 1995 and 2005, the DLB '
             'Consortium issued three consensus reports on DLB. DLB was included in the fourth '
             'text revision of the DSM (<a class="mw-redirect" href="DSM-IV-TR" '
             'title="DSM-IV-TR">DSM-IV-TR</a>, published in 2000) under "Dementia due to other '
             'general medical conditions". In the 2010s, the possibility of a genetic basis for '
             'LBD began to emerge. The Fourth Consensus Report was issued in 2017, giving '
             'increased diagnostic weighting to RBD and <span class="nowrap">I-MIBG</span> '
             'myocardial scintigraphy. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Society_and_culture">Society and culture</h2></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Lewy_body_dementia#Society_and_culture" title="Lewy body dementia">Lewy body '
             'dementia §\xa0Society and culture</a></div> <figure class="mw-default-size '
             'mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="See caption." '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="451" data-file-width="355" '
             'decoding="async" height="292" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Robin_Williams_Happy_Feet_premiere.jpg" width="230"/><figcaption>According to '
             'his widow, Robin Williams (pictured in 2011) was diagnosed during autopsy as having '
             'diffuse Lewy bodies.</figcaption></figure> <p>The British author and poet Mervyn '
             'Peake died in 1968 and was diagnosed <a href="Retrospective_diagnosis" '
             'title="Retrospective diagnosis">posthumously</a> as a probable case of DLB in a 2003 '
             'study published in <i>JAMA Neurology</i>. Based on signs in his work and letters of '
             'progressive deterioration, fluctuating cognitive decline, deterioration in '
             'visuospatial function, declining attention span, and visual hallucinations and '
             'delusions, his may be the earliest known case where DLB was found to have been the '
             'likely cause of death. </p><p>At the time of his suicide on August 11, 2014, Robin '
             "Williams, the American actor and comedian, had been diagnosed with Parkinson's "
             'disease. According to his widow, Williams had experienced depression, anxiety, and '
             'increasing paranoia. His widow said that his autopsy found diffuse Lewy body '
             'disease, while the autopsy used the term <i>diffuse Lewy body dementia</i>. Dennis '
             'Dickson, a spokesperson for the Lewy Body Dementia Association, clarified the '
             'distinction by stating that <i>diffuse Lewy body dementia</i> is more commonly '
             'called <i>diffuse Lewy body disease</i> and refers to the underlying disease '
             'process. According to Dickson, "Lewy bodies are generally limited in distribution, '
             'but in DLB, the Lewy bodies are spread widely throughout the brain, as was the case '
             'with Robin Williams." Ian G. McKeith, professor and researcher of Lewy body '
             "dementias, commented that Williams' symptoms and autopsy findings were explained by "
             'DLB. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Research_directions">Research '
             'directions</h2></div> <p>The identification of prodromal biomarkers for DLB will '
             'enable treatments to begin sooner, improve the ability to select subjects and '
             'measure efficacy in clinical trials, and help families and clinicians plan for early '
             'interventions and awareness of potential adverse effects from the use of '
             'antipsychotics. Criteria were established in 2020 to help researchers better '
             'recognize DLB in the pre-dementia phase. Three syndromes of prodromal DLB have been '
             'proposed: 1) mild cognitive impairment with Lewy bodies (MCI-LB); 2) delirium-onset '
             'DLB; and 3) psychiatric-onset DLB. The three early syndromes may overlap. As of '
             "2020, the DLB Diagnostic Study Group's position is that criteria for MCI-LB can be "
             'recommended, but that it remains difficult to distinguish delirium-onset and '
             'psychiatric-onset DLB without better biomarkers. Nonetheless, severe late-onset '
             'psychiatric disorders can be an indication to consider Lewy body dementia, and '
             'unexplained delirium raises the possibility of prodromal DLB. </p><p>The diagnosis '
             'of DLB is made using the DLB Consortium criteria, but a 2017 study of skin samples '
             'from 18 people with DLB found that all of them had deposits of phosphorylated '
             'alpha-synuclein, while none of the controls did, suggesting that skin samples offer '
             'diagnostic potential. Other potential biomarkers under investigation are <a '
             'href="Quantitative_electroencephalography" title="Quantitative '
             'electroencephalography">quantitative electroencephalography</a>, imaging examination '
             'of brain structures, and measures of synucleinopathy in <a '
             'href="Cerebrospinal_fluid" title="Cerebrospinal fluid">CSF</a>. While commercial '
             'skin biopsy tests for DLB are available in the US, and the FDA has given a <a '
             'href="Breakthrough_therapy" title="Breakthrough therapy">breakthrough '
             'authorization</a> for CSF testing, these tests are not widely available and their '
             'role in clinical practice has not been established as of 2022. Other tests to detect '
             'alpha-synuclein with blood tests and seed amplication assays are underway as of '
             '2025. </p><p><a class="mw-redirect" href="Cognitive_training" title="Cognitive '
             'training">Cognitive training</a>, <a href="Deep_brain_stimulation" title="Deep brain '
             'stimulation">deep brain stimulation</a> and <a '
             'href="Transcranial_direct-current_stimulation" title="Transcranial direct-current '
             'stimulation">transcranial direct-current stimulation</a> have been studied more in '
             "Parkinson's and Alzheimer's disease than they have in dementia with Lewy bodies, and "
             'all are potential therapies for DLB. Four clinical trials for treating parkinsonian '
             'symptoms in DLB have been completed as of 2021, but more studies are needed to '
             'assess risk vs. benefits, adverse effects, and longer-term therapeutic protocols. '
             '</p><p>Strategies for future interventions involve modifying the course of the '
             'disease using <a href="Immunotherapy" title="Immunotherapy">immunotherapy</a>, <a '
             'href="Gene_therapy" title="Gene therapy">gene therapy</a>, and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Stem_cell_therapy" title="Stem cell therapy">stem cell '
             'therapy</a>, and reducing amyloid beta and alpha-synuclein accumulation. Therapies '
             'under study as of 2019 aim to reduce brain levels of alpha-synuclein (with the '
             'pharmaceuticals <a href="Ambroxol" title="Ambroxol">ambroxol</a>, NPT200-11, and '
             'E2027), or to use immunotherapy to reduce widespread neuroinflammation resulting '
             'from alpha-synuclein deposits. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Notes">Notes</h2></div> <div class="reflist reflist-columns '
             'references-column-width reflist-lower-alpha" style="column-width: 32em;"> <ol '
             'class="references"> <li id="cite_note-fnAreas-19"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ '
             '<a href="#cite_ref-fnAreas_19-0"></a> <a href="#cite_ref-fnAreas_19-1"></a></span> '
             '<span class="reference-text">Areas of the brain and functions affected: <ul><li><a '
             'href="Cerebral_cortex" title="Cerebral cortex">cerebral cortex</a>\xa0– thought, '
             'perception and language;</li> <li><a href="Entorhinal_cortex" title="Entorhinal '
             'cortex">limbic cortex</a>\xa0– emotions and behavior;</li> <li><a href="Hippocampus" '
             'title="Hippocampus">hippocampus</a>\xa0– memory;</li> <li><a href="Midbrain" '
             'title="Midbrain">midbrain</a> and <a href="Basal_ganglia" title="Basal '
             'ganglia">basal ganglia</a>\xa0– movement;</li> <li><a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Brain_stem" title="Brain stem">brain stem</a>\xa0– sleep, alertness, and <a '
             'href="Autonomic_nervous_system" title="Autonomic nervous system">autonomic</a> '
             'dysfunction;</li> <li><a class="mw-redirect" href="Olfactory_cortex" '
             'title="Olfactory cortex">olfactory cortex</a>\xa0– smell.</li></ul> Also affected '
             'are the <a href="Hypothalamus" title="Hypothalamus">hypothalamus</a>, <a '
             'href="Spinal_cord" title="Spinal cord">spinal cord</a> and <a '
             'href="Peripheral_nervous_system" title="Peripheral nervous system">peripheral '
             'nervous system</a>—autonomic dysfunction.</span> </li> <li id="cite_note-122"><span '
             'class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-122">^</a></b></span> <span '
             'class="reference-text">The European Federation of Neurological Societies—European '
             'Neurological Society and the British Association for Psychopharmacology also have '
             'diagnostic guidelines, but they were not developed specifically for DLB, hence the '
             'DLB Consortium guidelines are the most widely used and cited.</span> </li> <li '
             'id="cite_note-fnSleepQues-131"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a '
             'href="#cite_ref-fnSleepQues_131-0">^</a></b></span> <span '
             'class="reference-text">Questionnaires such as the REM Sleep Behavior Disorder '
             'Screening Questionnaire (RBDSQ), the REM Sleep Behavior Questionnaires – Hong-Kong '
             '(RBD-HK), the Mayo Sleep Questionnaire (MSQ), the Innsbruck REM Sleep Behavior '
             'Disorder Inventory, and the <a '
             'href="REM_Sleep_Behavior_Disorder_Single-Question_Screen" title="REM Sleep Behavior '
             'Disorder Single-Question Screen">REM Sleep Behavior Disorder Single-Question '
             'Screen</a> are well-validated.</span> </li> <li id="cite_note-CommonDem-214"><span '
             'class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-CommonDem_214-0">^</a></b></span> '
             '<span class="reference-text">Kosaka (2017) writes: "Dementia with Lewy bodies (DLB) '
             'is now well known to be the second most frequent dementia following Alzheimer '
             'disease (AD). Of all types of dementia, AD is known to account for about 50%, DLB '
             'about 20% and vascular dementia (VD) about 15%. Thus, AD, DLB, and VD are now '
             'considered to be the three major dementias." Hershey (2019) says, "DLB is the third '
             "most common of all the neurodegenerative diseases behind both Alzheimer's disease "
             'and Parkinson\'s disease". Tsamakis &amp; Mueller (2021) say that DLB is the "second '
             'most common form of neurodegenerative dementia", and Armstrong (2021) says that '
             '"Lewy body dementia is the second-most common degenerative dementia after '
             'Alzheimer\'s disease (AD), but DLB is only one part of this diagnostic umbrella." '
             'The Lewy body dementias are also as a group the second most common form of '
             "neurodegenerative dementia after Alzheimer's disease (AD) as of 2021. </span> </li> "
             '</ol></div> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '

             'class="mw-page-container"> <div class="mw-page-container-inner"> <div '
             'class="mw-content-container"> <main class="mw-body" id="content"> <header '
             'class="mw-body-header vector-page-titlebar"> <h1 class="firstHeading '
             'mw-first-heading" id="firstHeading"> <span class="mw-page-title-main" '
             'id="openzim-page-title"><span class="mw-page-title-main">Progressive supranuclear '
             'palsy</span></span> </h1> </header> <a id="top"></a> <div '
             'aria-labelledby="firstHeading" class="vector-body '
             've-init-mw-desktopArticleTarget-targetContainer" data-mw-ve-target-container="" '
             'id="bodyContent"> <div class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" dir="ltr" '
             'id="mw-content-text" lang="en"><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" '
             'dir="ltr" lang="en"> <p class="mw-empty-elt"> </p> <table class="infobox '
             'infobox-has-images-with-white-backgrounds"><tbody><tr><th class="infobox-above" '
             'colspan="2" style="background:#ccc">Progressive supranuclear palsy</th></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">Other names</th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Steele–Richardson–Olszewski syndrome, frontotemporal dementia '
             'with parkinsonism</td></tr><tr><td class="infobox-full-data" colspan="2"><span '
